## Introduction

Going forward, new issues in the new template at the bottom of this file, will go in the '## New format' section only. No more status emojis. Refer to '## Reference' for sort order. Issues in the old format (with status emojis) won't be refactored, but will continue to be worked until moved to closed, canceled, or deferred sections, and emojis updated. (Eventually this will all be moved to nano-git-db anyway. This new template is an intermediate effort to make issues going forward more structured and importable.)

## Issues

- FreeBSD kernel panics while zuid runs.
	- ID: 2026100413383471
	- Type: Bug
	- Status: Can't reproduce
	- Needs external testing: No. Reopen if it comes back.
	- Priority [Feature|Enhancement] | Severity [Bug]: High
	- Opened: 20261004-133835
	- Opened by: JC
	- Parent ID: 2026100413383571
	- Target OS: FreeBSD 15.1, x86_64
	- Test environment: `vmFreeBSD`, 8 CPUs, 8 GB.
	- Version and build: 1.0.0-alpha.1 (build ddg8r), from the `freebsd` branch.
	- Steps to reproduce [Bug]:
		- Run `cli-test.bash --bin <tarball>/bin/zuid` on the VM.
	- Incorrect behavior [Bug]: The kernel panicked about two minutes in: "Fatal trap 12: page fault while in kernel mode", current process zuid. The backtrace runs from a user page fault through `vm_fault` and `vm_page_activate` into `vm_page_pqstate_commit`.
	- Expected behavior [Bug]: No program can panic the kernel. At worst zuid crashes.
	- Reproduced [Bug]: No. It happened once, 20261004, and the test log was lost in the reboot. 40 more runs of `cli-test.bash` did not bring it back: 16 one after another, and 24 four at a time with the Zig tests between them, with every zuid call logged to disk.
	- Possible cause [Bug]: A FreeBSD kernel bug in its page-queue code, reached by Wasmtime's large memory reservations and guard pages. A program cannot cause a kernel page fault by itself. FreeBSD has a page-queue race of the same kind open on powerpc64le under heavy load, bug 296767; not shown to be the same one.
	- Progress log:
		- 20261004: The crash dump is `/var/crash/vmcore.0` on the VM.
		- 20261004: Before it, the tarball's command, the 30 Zig tests and the C smoke test, static and shared, all passed there.
		- 20261004: The 40 runs above passed, 69 of 69 each, and the 24 Zig test runs passed.
		- 20261004: The VM was already current at 15.1-RELEASE-p4, the level it panicked on, so the update found nothing. 40 more runs the same way, against the release from 7dbdc21, passed 69 of 69 each, with 24 Zig test runs passing. No panic. See 2026100415162238.
	- Decisions:
		- Not a reason to hold the FreeBSD release, since zuid cannot cause or fix a kernel fault. Reopen if it comes back.
	- Branch: freebsd
	- Test case: none. The fault is in the kernel, and 80 runs did not bring it back.
	- Acceptance signoff: Closed as asked, 20261005.
	- Closed: 20261005-102900

- A run with a missing docker leaves an empty `tmp.*` file in TMPDIR.
	- ID: 2026101912130117
	- Type: Bug
	- Status: Can't reproduce
	- Priority [Feature|Enhancement] | Severity [Bug]: Low
	- Opened: 20261019-121301
	- Opened by: JC
	- Related IDs: 2026101816474402
	- Steps to reproduce [Bug]:
		- Run `cicd.bash --quick --leakcheck` with `ZUID_DOCKER` naming a program that doesn't exist.
	- Incorrect behavior [Bug]: An empty `tmp.*` file is left behind in TMPDIR.
	- Expected behavior [Bug]: Every temp file the run makes is gone when it ends.
	- Reproduced [Bug]: Seen once, 20261019. Not traced.
	- Progress log:
		- 20261019: The filed command, `ZUID_DOCKER=no-such-docker cicd.bash --only zig --quick --leakcheck`, left nothing in a TMPDIR of its own. Neither did a run of both sides with a missing docker named by path, or a run that failed partway. A watch on the folder saw no plain `tmp.*` file made at its top level in any of them, even for a moment. Every `tmp.*` folder was gone at the end.
		- 20261019: Every `mktemp` in the tree is a `mktemp -d` that goes on the cleanup list. Nothing calls a plain `mktemp`. The file seen was in the temp root every session on this box shares, and was tied to the run only by its time, about 34 s after the run started.
	- Branch: cicdtidy
	- Test case: none. Nothing reproduced it, so there's no failure for a test to pin. ddx66 and ddx67 already pin the cleanup of scratch folders.

- The Windows exe and dll differ between machines building one commit.
	- ID: 2026101305595483
	- Type: Bug
	- Status: Stalled
	- Priority|Severity [Bug]: Avg
	- Opened: 20261013-055954
	- Opened by: JC
	- Parent ID: 2026101304122319
	- Target OS: Windows
	- Steps to reproduce [Bug]:
		- Run `cicd.bash --package`, keep `dist/`, run `cicd.bash --container --package`, and compare `zuid.exe` and `zuid.dll` in the Windows zips.
	- Incorrect behavior [Bug]: They differ in 20 bytes, the time stamp and the PDB's ID.
	- Expected behavior [Bug]: The same bytes from any machine that builds the same commit.
	- Reproduced [Bug]: Yes, 20261013. A one-line Zig program built for x86_64-windows-gnu in 2 folders with different names gave 2 exes 20 bytes apart. With `-fstrip` they matched.
	- Possible cause [Bug]: Zig links with `-BREPRO`, so lld makes the PDB's ID from a hash of the PDB, and the time stamp from that. The PDB has the absolute paths of the checkout, the Zig install and its cache. Zig has no way to map those paths yet. Upstream: ziglang/zig#31001 on Codeberg, debug-prefix-map support, open.
	- Progress log:
		- 20261013: Stalled on ziglang/zig#31001, a path map for debug info.
		- 20261013: Ruled out: lld's `/pdbsourcepath` rewrites relative paths only. A build with no PDB matches, but 2026101008194330 keeps the PDB in `dist/debug/` so a crash can be looked up. Patching the ID after the link is a hack.
		- 20261013: Paths aren't the only cause. 2 cold builds at one path still gave 2 PDBs. Zig builds its mingw C runtime from temp files with random names, such as `tmp/679bb39054fc1352-crt2.obj`, and clang puts each command line in the PDB. 88 such names per PDB. No upstream issue found for that.

- vmKt6m isn't put back to off after the Mac box step.
	- ID: 2026102617014623
	- Type: Bug
	- Status: Done
	- Priority [Feature|Enhancement] | Severity [Bug]: Avg
	- Opened: 20261026-170146
	- Opened by: JC
	- Related IDs: 2026102517494666, 2026102416233100
	- Target OS: macOS
	- Steps to reproduce [Bug]:
		- With vmKt6m off and b26 not answering, run the Mac box step.
	- Incorrect behavior [Bug]: The helper's ACPI shutdown is ignored, so it gives up after 300 s, with vmKt6m still running and its note kept. The step passes, with a note that the box may not be back the way it was found.
	- Expected behavior [Bug]: vmKt6m is off again after the step, with no wait.
	- Reproduced [Bug]: 20261026, with the Mac box step alone and the helper's stop wait cut to 60 s.
	- Possible cause [Bug]: The macOS guest ignores the ACPI power request. A `sudo -n shutdown -h now` from inside works.
	- Progress log:
		- 20261026: The drafted helper change shuts vmKt6m down from inside over ssh, as `collierjr`, in place of the ACPI request. Run once by hand on vmKt6m after the Mac box step left it up: off in 15 s, note cleared.
		- 20261026: The change is in the shared helper now. Its `restore` put vmKt6m back to off from a running state with a note in 12 s.
	- Actual fix [Bug]: The VM state helper shuts vmKt6m down over ssh. Nothing changed in zuid.
	- Test case: none. The fix is in the shared helper, outside the repo, and the box step only calls it.
	- Acceptance signoff: Self-closed: the helper's `restore` turned vmKt6m off with no wait.
	- Closed: 20261026-202617

- convertbase's config parser turns a 32-bit number into an `int`, which can wrap on a 32-bit build.
	- ID: 2026101911183280
	- Type: Bug
	- Status: Stalled
	- Priority [Feature|Enhancement] | Severity [Bug]: Low
	- Opened: 20261019-111832
	- Opened by: JC
	- Related IDs: 2026101816474402, 2026100519164742
	- Incorrect behavior [Bug]: `parseU32` in convert-base-v2's `lib/shcl/shcl.go`, at the pinned `lib/v0.1.0`, parses up to 4294967295 and returns it as an `int`. On a 32-bit build anything over 2147483647 wraps negative. CodeQL's `go/incorrect-integer-conversion` found it.
	- Expected behavior [Bug]: Nothing past the `int` range comes back as an `int`.
	- Reproduced [Bug]: No. Read only. Its one caller reads a day of the month and refuses anything outside 1 to 31, which a wrapped value is, so no wrong result was found. zuid never loads a config.
	- Progress log:
		- 20261019: The code is upstream, so it's fixed in convert-base-v2 and comes in with a later pin. Until then `cicd/utility/codeql-suppressions.txt` suppresses it by this ID.
		- 20261019: Stalled with the pin move, 2026100519164742.

- A fresh full run fails: the FreeBSD `libwasmtime-49.0.1` package no longer matches its pinned checksum.
	- ID: 2026102519140200
	- Type: Bug
	- Status: Done
	- Needs local test suite run?: none.
	- Priority [Feature|Enhancement] | Severity [Bug]: High
	- Opened: 20261025-191402
	- Opened by: JC
	- Related IDs: 2026101410500001, 2026101218572197
	- Target OS: FreeBSD
	- Bug details:
		- Step 14, Zig: vendor, stops with "FreeBSD libwasmtime-49.0.1 checksum mismatch: got b2a632b8e9652060197d63bd8dcd6052a1c821b0253e6b6049768adf9ccd494e". The pin in `cicd.bash` is 95ee2302...
		- Seen 20261025 in a full run from a fresh worktree at 897fa59. A checkout with the package already vendored doesn't fetch it, so it passes there.
		- Same version, new bytes, so the package server rebuilt it. Every fresh packaging run fails until the pin is checked and moved, or checked some other way that survives a rebuild.
	- Reproduced [Bug]: Yes, 20261025. zstd-1.5.7_2 was rebuilt too, to `baa629f0...`. The run stopped at libwasmtime first.
	- Actual cause [Bug]:
		- The FreeBSD 14 quarterly repo rebuilt both packages under the same versions, on 2026-10-15 and 10-17. The new files are FreeBSD's own: the repo's catalog is signed with the key in FreeBSD's `share/keys/pkg/trusted`, and its sums match both files.
		- The libraries inside are byte for byte the ones vendored on 20261013. Only the package wrapping changed.
		- The catalog's `sum` is pkg's `2$` form: the file's blake2b-512 in pkg's own base32. The 20261012 note that it wasn't a file hash was wrong.
	- Actual fix [Bug]: The pins are versions now, not file hashes. The vendor step fetches the catalog, checks its key and signature, then checks each package against its entry there. A version the repo has moved past says to repin. A tree already vendored at the same versions is kept.
	- Decisions:
		- 20261025: Checked against the signed catalog rather than repinned. A repin breaks again at the next rebuild, and this is the second break in two weeks. The cost is that the bytes can change without a commit. The release stays pinned to a Wasmtime version, and to FreeBSD's key.
		- 20261025: openssl and b2sum are needed on the Linux box that packages. The image already has both, so the Dockerfile is unchanged.
	- Branch: round1026
	- Test case: deebc (packages the catalog vouches for are taken, a rebuild included, and pkg's sum form against known vectors) and deebd (a moved version, a dep's version taken for the package's, a changed package in either sum form, an edited catalog and another key are refused). Both failed against the old `cicd.bash`, and deebd failed with the signature check or the version read taken out.
	- Verified:
		- 20261025: The vendor step from a fresh folder against the real repo: both packages matched the signed catalog, and the vendored tree matched the cached one byte for byte.
		- 20261025: `pipeline-test.bash` 165 of 165, `test-ids.py check`, and shellcheck on both scripts.
		- Not verified: the step inside a full run.
	- Swept: every `freebsdPkgs` and `freebsdRepo` read in `cicd.bash`, and design.md's line on the FreeBSD pin. `fVendor_Wasmtime` pins Wasmtime's own release archives by sha256, which GitHub serves unchanged, so it stays as it is.
	- Closed: 20261025-203628
	- 20261025: Verified in the full run log of 20261025-200553_004 at da19f12, from a fresh worktree so the vendor step fetched: ddgey to ddgf2 and dee33 PASS.

- dedpa fails in the image, which has no `ps`.
	- ID: 2026102508540624
	- Type: Bug
	- Status: Done
	- Priority [Feature|Enhancement] | Severity [Bug]: High
	- Opened: 20261025-085406
	- Opened by: JC
	- Related IDs: 2026102416233102, 2026102416233104, 2026102509054739
	- Target OS: Linux
	- Steps to reproduce [Bug]:
		- Run `cicd/cicd.bash --backlog-run-quick --only go` where docker answers.
	- Incorrect behavior [Bug]: The Pipeline step fails in the image on dedpa, "ps: command not found", so the run stops there.
	- Expected behavior [Bug]: It passes in the image, as on the host.
	- Reproduced [Bug]: Yes, 20261025, on b23, twice, while working 2026102416233104.
	- Possible cause [Bug]: The test reads the nice value with `ps`, and the image has no procps. Preflight's own priority line uses `ps` too, with a fallback to nothing.
	- Actual cause [Bug]:
		- As above. The image has `renice`, `ionice` and `nice`, but no `ps`, so the renice worked and only the reading of it failed. Preflight in the image printed a blank nice value.
	- Actual fix [Bug]: `fNiceOf` in `cicd.bash` reads the nice value with `ps` where there is one, and from `/proc/<pid>/stat` where there isn't. Preflight and dedpa both use it. dedpa also checks the `/proc` branch wherever `/proc` exists, with `ps` hidden. Preflight says `unknown` rather than nothing when neither works.
	- Swept: every `ps` in `cicd/` and `.github/`. The only others are `docker ps`, and a stub of it in `pipeline-test.bash`.
	- Branch: round1025-fix
	- Commit: 26605ac
	- Test case: dedpa. It failed in the image before the fix, "ps: command not found", and passes after. With the `/proc` field index broken by hand it fails on the host too, "19|39|idle".
	- Verified: 20261025, `pipeline-test.bash` 128 of 128 on the host and in `zuid-cicd:8029eec721c8`. `cicd.bash --backlog-run-quick --only go` in the image passed, steps 1 to 14 of 14, with "Priority ...: nice 19, IO idle" from inside the image, at load 44. shellcheck, `test-ids.py check` and the scrub are clean.
	- Closed: 20261025-090547

- Release binaries keep debug info and the build machine's paths.
	- ID: 2026101008194330
	- Type: Bug
	- Status: Done
	- Needs external testing: Done. b26 and b29w, 20261013, see Verified.
	- Priority|Severity [Bug]: High
	- Opened: 20261010-081943
	- Opened by: JC
	- Related IDs: 2026101008194270
	- Target OS: Any
	- Steps to reproduce [Bug]:
		- `strings -a bin/zuid` from the `v1.0.0-alpha.1` Linux x86_64 tarball.
	- Incorrect behavior [Bug]: 48 paths under the builder's home directory, from the Zig install and Zig's cache. The file is 28.4 MB and has 10 debug sections.
	- Expected behavior [Bug]: No paths from the build machine, and the same bytes from any machine that builds the same commit.
	- Reproduced [Bug]: Yes, 20261010, in the published alpha and in builds from b23 and the container. Stripped, the alpha binary is 18.7 MB with none of those paths.
	- Possible cause [Bug]: Release builds keep their debug info, and nothing strips it.
	- Actual cause [Bug]:
		- Confirmed. Nothing stripped the release builds, so each kept its DWARF, with the paths of the Zig install, its caches, the checkout and the home dir. The static libraries had the same. The Windows zips had the PDB too.
	- Progress log:
		- 20261010: found while comparing a container build with a host build. Every release file differed.
		- 20261013: Reproduced on b23 by the new check, 43 problems, the same paths and debug sections in every binary of every asset.
		- 20261013: Stripped, the bare Linux and FreeBSD binaries come out the same from b23 and the container. Other files still differ for reasons outside debug info, logged as 2026101304122319.
	- Decisions:
		- 20261013: release binaries are stripped. Each one's debug info goes in `dist/debug/`, not published, but kept by the backup so a panic address can still be looked up. The Windows `.pdb` leaves the zips the same way.
		- 20261013: `libzuid.a` keeps only its debug info in `dist/debug/`, not a whole unstripped copy.
	- Actual fix [Bug]: `package.bash` strips every binary built here. On Linux that's `llvm-objcopy`, for both ELF and Windows files. On a Mac it's `strip`, with `dsymutil` first. A program or shared library keeps its symbols and DWARF in `dist/debug/<release>/`, as `<name>.debug` or `<name>.dSYM`. A static library keeps its symbols, and only its DWARF goes there, as `libzuid.a.debug`. On Linux every ELF `.debug` file is zlib packed, which gdb reads as is. A Mac keeps a whole unstripped `libzuid.a`, since Apple's tools can't split an archive. Both Windows PDBs move there, the DLL's included, which was dropped before. The AVX and armv8-a scans take function names from the debug file.
	- Branch: strip, dbgonly
	- Commit: 60169a5, f9cd9fd
	- Test case: ddwj4, ddwj5, ddwj6 and ddwx7.
		- ddwj4 fails on any file in the release with this machine's paths, any binary built here with debug sections, and any PDB. It failed on the tree before the fix, with 43 problems.
		- ddwj5 checks each binary has its own debug info in `dist/debug/`. It failed with another release's `.debug` file, the command's PDB in place of the DLL's, and an archive copy missing.
		- ddwj6 checks nothing under `dist/debug/` is uploaded or hashed by `--publish`. It and ddfbm failed with the publish stage reading all of `dist/`.
		- ddwx7 checks each ELF file in `dist/debug/` has packed DWARF and no code. It failed on all 9 from the run before, the 3 whole archive copies among them.
	- Swept: every asset in `dist/`, the bare binaries, tarballs, zips, `.deb` and `.rpm`, with the shared and static libraries. The scans that read names: dde03, ddgez, ddfaa and ddgpb read the debug files now. ddw1c reads the unstripped dogfood build and is unchanged. `--publish` and the checksums in `package.bash` read only the top of `dist/`.
	- Verified: `cicd.bash --package` passed on b23 and with `--container`, 20261013. The Linux x86_64 command is 18.8 MB, down from 28.9 MB. Through the debug file, the AVX scan found the same 235 functions as on the unstripped build, for a build for this CPU. The arm64 scan found the same 56 on a cortex_a76 build.
	- Verified: 20261013, b26 `--package` at 2fa8524, with dde03 and ddwmp skipped since both fail there for other items: ddwj4, ddwj5, ddf3k, dde05 and ddf2d pass, and the stripped universal command runs. The full b23 run passed too.
	- Verified: 20261013, b29w, the stripped x86_64 zip under pwsh 7: `zuid.exe` ran every component, a cold and a warm module cache, base 2048tz and a salt, and refused a bad base. `capi_smoke.c` passed with mingw gcc against `zuid.dll` and against `libzuid.a`. On b23 `dist/debug/` went from 127 MB to 72 MB a run, and `cicd.bash --package` passed.
	- Note: `codesign -v` calls the command's x86_64 slice unsigned. An Intel Mac doesn't need one. Whether it was signed before the strip wasn't checked.
	- Acceptance signoff: Self-closed: tested on every platform but arm64 Windows, which nothing here can run.
	- Closed: 20261013-094815

- The dogfood install puts a build for the build machine's CPU in the synced dir.
	- ID: 2026101218531694
	- Type: Bug
	- Status: Done
	- Priority|Severity [Bug]: High
	- Opened: 20261012-185316
	- Opened by: JC
	- Related IDs: 2026093018112406
	- Target OS: Linux
	- Steps to reproduce [Bug]:
		- Run a full `cicd.bash` on a newer x86_64 box. Run `zuid -f %u` from the synced dir on one without SHA-NI, such as an i7-6600U.
	- Incorrect behavior [Bug]: Illegal instruction, in Zig's SHA-256, on the first hash. Anything with no hashing in it still works.
	- Expected behavior [Bug]: Whatever the synced dir holds runs on every x86_64 box that reads it.
	- Reproduced [Bug]: Yes, on b28l. `v1.0.0-alpha.1` does the same, since it came before 2026093018112406.
	- Actual cause [Bug]:
		- 2026093018112406 named a target in `package.bash` only. The Zig stage's own build, which dogfood copies, still targets the host CPU.
		- The synced dir is first in the dogfood list on any OS, and it syncs to the Mac and the arm64 VM too.
	- Actual fix [Bug]: On Linux the Zig stage builds the command for `package.bash`'s target. Only a Linux x86_64 run puts it in the synced dir; others start at `~/.local/bin`.
	- Branch: dogfoodcpu
	- Test case: ddw1c, the AVX scan on the build dogfood copies. Failed on a build for the host: 235 functions used AVX.
	- Verified: 20261013, the synced copy's only SHA-NI code is in Wasmtime's Rust `sha2`, which asks the CPU first. None in Zig's SHA-256.
	- Acceptance signoff: Self-closed: tested, and the old box can't easily be reached for a rerun.
	- Closed: 20261013-033301

- The macOS build hangs on Zig 0.17.0.
	- ID: 2026100318410002
	- Type: Bug
	- Status: Done
	- Needs local test suite run?: No. A full `cicd.bash --no-dogfood --no-backup --cross -q` passed on Linux with the fix.
	- Needs external testing: Done on b26, 20261004.
	- Priority|Severity [Bug]: High
	- Opened: 20261003-184100
	- Opened by: JC
	- Related IDs: 2026100313583935, 2026100415475381
	- Target OS: macOS
	- Steps to reproduce [Bug]:
		- On b26, run `cicd.bash` with Zig 0.17.0.
		- Or by hand: `zig translate-c -lc <any header> | wc -c`.
	- Incorrect behavior [Bug]: The Zig stage never ends. The three header steps sit at full CPU.
	- Expected behavior [Bug]: The build finishes, as it does on Linux.
	- Reproduced [Bug]: Yes, on b26, 20261003. Even a one-line header hangs when the output goes to a pipe. The same command writing to a file finishes at once.
	- Possible cause [Bug]: Zig copies its output to stdout with `fcopyfile`, which fails on a pipe there, and Zig retries forever. The header step reads stdout through a pipe. No upstream report found.
	- Progress log:
		- 20261003: Untested idea: have the step write to its output file through `sh -c`, so Zig never writes to a pipe.
		- 20261004: Reproduced again on b26: piped, a one-line header gave no output in 20 s; to a file it finished at once. `zig translate-c` has no output file option, so the step cannot just name one.
		- 20261004: Not `sh -c`, since a native Windows build has no `sh`. A small Zig helper does the redirect on every host instead.
		- 20261004: Found along the way: `dcrga` could not see the header step at all. The translator kept its own cache beside `build.zig`, so the test's fresh cache still got a warm translation. Fixed here, since the test pins what this step must keep.
		- 20261004: Also found: a cold global cache fails the empty `SOURCE_DATE_EPOCH` build anyway, in Zig's own libunwind build. Same on `main`. Logged as 2026100415475381.
	- Actual cause [Bug]: `zig translate-c` copies its cached result to stdout with `fcopyfile` on macOS. Read in Zig's source, not traced: an error that is not "unsupported" makes the copy report zero bytes, and the caller asks again forever. Linux takes another path.
	- Actual fix [Bug]: The three header steps run `zig translate-c` through `zig/tools/to_file.zig`, which gives it a file for stdout. Still a Run step, so it still drops `SOURCE_DATE_EPOCH`. The translator now gets the build's cache directory too.
	- Verified: on b26, a fresh-cache build finished in about 23 s, a fresh-cache build with `SOURCE_DATE_EPOCH` empty finished too, and all 30 Zig tests passed. That copy had no git history, so it had no build number to check. On Linux, the translated headers match `zig translate-c` byte for byte, the arm64 Linux and FreeBSD cross builds work, a no-op rebuild takes under a tenth of a second, and the full pipeline passes.
	- Swept: `captureStdOut` and `addSystemCommand` in `build.zig`, and every `zig` call in the scripts. The header steps were the only place Zig wrote a file to a pipe. `cicd.bash`'s `zig fmt` check now covers `zig/tools`.
	- Branch: machang
	- Commit: 47c1134
	- Test case: new `ddgkf` fails if `build.zig` captures a command's stdout. It fails on the old `build.zig` and passes now. A Linux build cannot hang, so the hang itself waits on the b26 run. `dcrga` now fails when the step stops dropping `SOURCE_DATE_EPOCH`; before the cache fix it passed either way.
	- Verified: 20261004 on b26, at 12d2b2d: the full pipeline with `--package` passed in about 7 minutes, with a fresh clone and a cold Zig cache for the project. The Zig stage finished, and `ddgkf` and `dcrga` passed. One test fixture line was changed for that run, for an unrelated failure logged as 2026100417561172.
	- Acceptance signoff: Self-closed: the macOS build finishes, and its tests pass.
	- Closed: 20261004-175611

- Release builds target the build machine's CPU.
	- ID: 2026093018112406
	- Type: Bug
	- Status: Done
	- Priority|Severity [Bug]: High
	- Opened: 20260930-181124
	- Opened by: JC
	- Target OS: Any
	- Steps to reproduce [Bug]:
		- `package.bash` runs `zig build` with no `-Dtarget` or `-Dcpu`.
	- Incorrect behavior [Bug]: Zig then builds for the host CPU, so a release binary can use instructions an older x86_64 lacks. On macOS it also targets the host's exact OS version.
	- Expected behavior [Bug]: A baseline CPU, and a stated minimum macOS.
	- Reproduced [Bug]: Yes. A Linux release used AVX in about 190 functions and needed glibc 2.36. A macOS one used AVX and asked for 15.8.
	- Actual cause [Bug]:
		- With no target named, Zig builds for the machine it runs on, glibc included.
	- Decisions:
		- Baseline CPU. The code built here is too small to gain from newer instructions, and Wasmtime checks the CPU itself.
	- Actual fix [Bug]: `package.bash` names a target: glibc 2.28 on Linux, macOS 13.0 on a Mac, both with the baseline CPU. It now builds the macOS tarball and binary on a Mac too.
	- Branch: baseline
	- Commit: 668b432
	- Test case: `dde03`, `dde04`, `dde05`, run with `--package`. Each watched to fail on a build for the host.
	- Closed: 20261002-194500

- `%m` is the same on every Intel Mac with a T2 chip.
	- ID: 2026093018112392
	- Type: Bug
	- Status: Done
	- Priority|Severity [Bug]: High
	- Opened: 20260930-181124
	- Opened by: JC
	- Target OS: macOS, Intel with a T2 chip
	- Steps to reproduce [Bug]:
		- Run `zuid --no-hash -b 16 -f %m`.
	- Incorrect behavior [Bug]: `ACDE48001122`. That is the T2 bridge interface, `en5`, and every T2 Mac has that address on it.
	- Expected behavior [Bug]: An address that differs between hosts.
	- Reproduced [Bug]: Yes, on macOS 15.8. Go and Zig agree.
	- Actual cause [Bug]:
		- The lowest-numbered interface wins, and `en5` comes before `en0` there.
	- Decisions:
		- Skip that one address on both sides. The lowest-numbered rule stays.
	- Actual fix [Bug]: Both sides skip `ac:de:48:00:11:22`. On the test Mac `%m` is now `ap1`'s address, index 5, which is ahead of `en0`. Both sides agree.
	- Branch: t2mac
	- Commit: 91411c1
	- Test case: `ddc8r`, `ddc8s`. Both watched to fail without the skip.
	- Closed: 20261001-135500

		- 20261025: `test-ids.py` encodes and decodes minutes in Crockford base 32. `new` skips minutes already taken in the tree, since `-n 5` now spans 5 minutes.
		- 20261025: The stray ID check now reads any 5-symbol word that decodes into range. Zig's `defer` decodes to 2026-10-26, so plain words like it are listed in `WORDS` in `test-ids.py`, and a word joined by `_` no longer counts.

		- 20261025: dedw3 checks `dan8z`, the minute before the first commit, not `decp8`, since a `decp8` written into a harness would be read as a stray ID.
	- Swept: every `ok ....:`, `FAIL ..:`, `skip ..:` and `???????` in `cicd/` and `.github/`, every 7-symbol pattern in the harnesses (the `--list` case had one), `lint-report.bash`, `development.md` and `design.md` (no line or ID format in either), and every tracked file for an old ID, none left.

- CICD reset: container cleanup
	- ID: 2026102416233101
	- Type: Enhancement
	- Status: Done
		- 20261025: The old unlabeled `zuid-cicd:856cd00870eb` and `zuid-codeql:de7763196c93-856cd00870eb` were removed by ID after the labeled pair passed.
	- Needs local test suite run?: Done. `cicd/cicd.bash --backlog-run-quick` at f123531 ended with one `Docker .....:` line, and so did the full run. After them, `zuid-cicd:8029eec721c8` and `zuid-codeql:de7763196c93-8029eec721c8` had both labels, `docker volume ls --filter label=dev.project=zuid` listed `zuid-cicd-cache` and `zuid-zig-cache`, and `docker ps -a --filter label=dev.project=zuid` listed nothing.
	- Needs external testing: Done. The `macos` workflow, run 40211475106 on `round1025-fix`, whose tree is round1025's head 45f01a1, passed, dedn9 included.
	- Priority [Feature|Enhancement] | Severity [Bug]: High
	- Opened: 20261024-162331
	- Opened by: JC
	- Related IDs: 2026101916041200, 2026102406590100
	- Requirements  [Feature]:
		- Sweeping override, 2026-10-24: this supersedes any earlier decision on this project that conflicts with it.
		- Per the container bullets in `Cleanup after a run` in the shared CICD rules. Containers already run with `--rm`.
		- Every container, image and volume the pipeline makes gets 2 labels, `dev.project=zuid` and `dev.run=<run folder>`. Containers are named `zuid-<run folder>-<step>`.
		- At the end of a run, on failure and Ctrl+C too, the run's containers are stopped and removed by label. `--keep-temp` keeps them.
		- Dangling images go by the project label. An older tag goes once a newer one has passed, and the current tag and the one before it stay. Today every other tag goes right after a build.
		- Then `docker builder prune -f --filter until=168h`, so a week of build cache stays warm.
		- The cache volume follows the same 2 GB rule as the Zig caches.
		- One line at the end says what was removed and about what it freed, from `docker system df` before and after.
		- What was built before the labels gets one pass by hand, by ID.
		- Earlier decisions this overrides: the 20261023 note on 2026101916041200 that docker's build cache stays with a prune by hand, and the private container notes, where a build drops every other `zuid-cicd` tag.
	- Progress log:
		- 20261025: Every container, image and volume a run makes has `dev.project=zuid` and `dev.run=<run>`. Containers are `zuid-<run>-<step>`, numbered per step. The two cache volumes are made labeled when missing, since docker makes them bare.
		- 20261025: At the end of a run, failed or interrupted too, its containers are stopped and removed by both labels. `--keep-temp` keeps them, and drops `--rm`.
		- 20261025: A run that passed then removes, by the project label only, every `zuid-codeql` and `zuid-cicd` tag but the current one and the one before it, CodeQL's first, and the dangling images. Then `docker builder prune -f --filter until=168h`, and `zuid-cicd-cache` is emptied when it's over 2048 MB. One `Docker .....:` line says what went and about what it freed.
		- 20261025: Images are no longer dropped right after a build.
		- 20261025: Hand pass: `zuid-cicd-cache` (421 MB, 336 MB of it the old Zig cache at `/cache/home/.cache/zig`) was removed, so the next run makes it labeled. No container used it.
		- 20261025: Left for a hand pass by ID: `zuid-cicd:856cd00870eb` (`6f89bc5604b3`) and `zuid-codeql:de7763196c93-856cd00870eb` (`d2cc10f16f9a`). They're unlabeled, so the rule never removes them, and `dev` and `main` still run on them. They go once a newer labeled image has passed.
		- 20261025: On b23 one `docker system df` call in a run came back empty, and the line said the whole of docker was freed. 12 calls in a row and 2 at once all answered, so the cause is not found. The line now says the amount is unknown when either call is empty.
		- 20261025: ddrj0 now wants no `rmi` during the stage, where it wanted `rmi zuid-cicd:0ld0ld0ld0ld`. Its build line, and those of de5ea and dedm0, have the two labels now.
		- 20261025: ddrj1's run line has `--name zuid-20261019-101112-container --label dev.project=zuid --label dev.run=20261019-101112` after the volumes now.
		- 20261025: de5ea and debk4 now want no `rmi` during the stage, where they wanted the old CodeQL tag, then the old main one. dednb checks that order at the end of the run instead.
		- 20261025: dedn9 failed on the `macos` workflow since c50557e. The 4 names were apart, but the Mac's `wc -l` pads its count with spaces, so the count never matched "4". It strips them now, like the other counts. With a padding `wc` on PATH it failed before and passes after, and `pipeline-test.bash` is 128 of 128 both ways.
		- Verified: `pipeline-test.bash` 115 of 115. With each of 4 faults put in, its own case failed: an unlabeled volume (dedn9), the one before not kept (dednb), `--keep-temp` ignored (dedna), and no call from the exit trap (dedna). Against a scratch label on b23's docker, the volumes came up labeled, a left-running container was stopped and removed, and the line was printed.
		- 20261025: The quick and full runs at f123531 passed in the image. Both ended with "removed build cache over a week old; about 0.0 GB freed". Only two labeled tags of each image exist, so none went.
	- Decisions:

- Zig's build caches grow without limit.
	- ID: 2026102320181236
	- Type: Enhancement
	- Status: Done
	- Needs local test suite run?: Done. `cicd/cicd.bash --backlog-run-quick` at f123531 passed in `zuid-cicd:8029eec721c8`, and so did the full run. After them, `docker volume ls` listed `zuid-zig-cache`, labeled, whose `.cicd-zig-version` says `0.17.0`, and `zig/.zig-cache` has the same stamp.
	- Priority [Feature|Enhancement] | Severity [Bug]: High
	- Opened: 20261023-201812
	- Opened by: JC
	- Parent ID: 2026101916041200
	- Requirements  [Feature]:
		- Zig never prunes its cache. On b23 `zig/.zig-cache` reached 711 MB in 5 days, and the Zig cache in the `zuid-cicd-cache` volume 336 MB. Go's build cache trims itself.
		- Zig's global cache in `~/.cache/zig` is shared with other projects and Zig versions, so it isn't ours to prune.
		- Sweeping override, 2026-10-24: this supersedes any earlier decision on this project that conflicts with it.
	- Progress log:
		- 20261024: Answered: (b). At the end of a run, `cicd.bash` empties `zig/.zig-cache`, or the volume's Zig cache under `--container`, when it's over about 2 GB. `~/.cache/zig` is left alone.
		- 20261024: Raised to high by the CICD reset, per the Zig bullets in `Cleanup after a run` in the shared CICD rules. A cache is also emptied when the Zig that filled it isn't the current one, from a stamp file inside it, and that goes for every `.zig-cache/` in the tree. In the container, Zig's global cache gets a volume of its own, `zuid-zig-cache`, under the same 2 GB rule. Always the whole folder.
		- 20261025: At the end of a run that built with Zig, every `.zig-cache` in the tree is emptied whole when it's over 2048 MB, or when its `.cicd-zig-version` names another Zig. Then it's stamped with this one. A linked cache is emptied through the link, and the link stays.
		- 20261025: The image sets `ZIG_GLOBAL_CACHE_DIR=/zig-cache`, and every container run mounts `zuid-zig-cache` there. The run inside tidies it with the tree's caches. The run outside built nothing, so it skips.
		- 20261025: ddrj1's run line has `-v zuid-zig-cache:/zig-cache` after `-v zuid-cicd-cache:/cache` now. It had no Zig volume before.
		- 20261025: The old global cache, 336 MB at `/cache/home/.cache/zig` in `zuid-cicd-cache`, is no longer used. It's left for the hand pass in 2026102416233101, and so are labels on the new volume.
		- Verified: `pipeline-test.bash` 112 of 112. In a build of the new image, Zig's global cache was `/zig-cache`, a new volume there was writable by the caller's uid, and the tidy emptied it over the cap and on a `0.16.0` stamp, and kept it otherwise. On b23 the tidy stamped `zig/.zig-cache`, 1.4 GB with no stamp, and left it whole.
		- 20261025: The round's quick and full runs passed in the image at f123531. The volume and the tree's cache were both stamped `0.17.0` after.
	- Decisions:
		- 20261025: A cache with no stamp was just filled by this run's Zig, so it gets a stamp and stays.
		- 20261025: The version check runs at the end, per the shared rule. So the first run on a new Zig ends with an empty cache, and the next one builds cold once.
		- 20261025: The pre-push gate's worktree uses the checkout's cache through `ZIG_LOCAL_CACHE_DIR`. That cache sits outside the worktree, so the checkout's own runs tidy it, not the gate.
		- 20261025: The size is what `du` reports, which on ZFS is the compressed size.
	- Swept: every docker run goes through `fContainer_RunArgs`, for the stage and for `fInImage`, so the volume reaches both. Only the container stage runs Zig in the image. Removal goes only through `fZigCache_Empty`. All 3 test rigs that set `containerVolume` set the Zig volume too.
	- Branch: round1025
	- Commit: deb8b3a
	- Test case: dedmt (over the cap, every cache in the tree, through a link, never `.git`), dedmv (another Zig's stamp, and none), dedmw (the image's volume, only inside), dedmx (the guard), dedmy (where fMain calls it). Each failed against the script before the change, and passes after.
	- Closed: 20261025-095700

- Updates to CICD script flags.

	- Type: Enhancement

		- 20261019: Reversed. `--ci` is `--gate` again, with no alias, in `cicd.bash`, its help, the pre-push hook, `pipeline-test.bash` and `development.md`. A hook that still runs `--ci` stops the push the same way the old one did.
		- 20261018: `--quick` runs every quick lint, check and test, plus the Go cross builds. It skips the Zig cross builds, which come with packaging, since they'd make `--ci` about 1.5 min longer.

- The C module keeps some choices per call and some on the context.
	- ID: 2026100519343982
	- Type: Enhancement
	- Status: Done
	- Priority [Feature|Enhancement] | Severity [Bug]: High
	- Opened: 20261005-193439
	- Opened by: JC
	- Target OS: Any
	- Requirements  [Feature]:
		- Format and base came with each call. Precision, hashing, the salt and both widths were set on the context and stayed set, so code sharing a context could change another part's output. Go takes all of them per call.
		- Settle it before the C ABI is held to at 1.0.0.
	- Progress log:
		- 20261005: `zuid_generate` takes a `zuid_request` with the same fields as Go's `Request`, and NULL is every default. The 5 setters are gone. The clock stays on the context, as Go keeps it on the `Generator`.
		- 20261005: a NULL or freed context returns the new `ZUID_ERR_CONTEXT`, 14. It used to be `ZUID_ERR_INTERNAL`, which says the runtime failed.
		- 20261005: a width the format does not use is not checked, as in Go. The setters refused one whatever the format.
	- Decisions:
		- 20261005: one call taking the struct, not a second call beside the old one. Two ways to do one thing is what was being fixed.
		- 20261005: the soname stays `libzuid.so.1`. The header now says the prereleases are not held to it. `v1.0.0-alpha.1` has the old call.
	- Branch: fastcli
	- Test case: daqq9, dcref, dav7b and the other C tests in `tests.zig` pass a request now, and so does `capi_smoke.c`. daqq9 failed with a negative width read as zero, and dav7b with the old code.
	- Acceptance signoff: Self-closed: matches Go's `Request`, as asked after the design review, and the prereleases aren't held to the ABI.

- A run of the command costs about 3.5 s of CPU and 235 MB of memory.
	- ID: 2026100519164741
	- Type: Enhancement
	- Status: Done
	- Needs external testing: Done, 20261005. Windows x86_64 on vm925w and macOS on b26.
	- Priority [Feature|Enhancement] | Severity [Bug]: High
	- Opened: 20261005-191647
	- Opened by: JC
	- Related IDs: 2026100519164742
	- Target OS: Any
	- Requirements  [Feature]:
		- One identifier takes microseconds. A run should not cost a compile of the whole wasm module every time.
	- Progress log:
		- 20261005: Measured on b23, ReleaseSafe: 0.55 s, 3.45 s of CPU and 235 MB per run. Cranelift compiles the module on every core, every run, and `_initialize` is most of the wall time.
		- 20261005: The command keeps the compiled module in the user's cache directory, and later runs load it. Wasmtime refuses one from another version or for another CPU, and then it compiles as usual. The file ends in a hash, so a damaged one is compiled over rather than run. A new build clears out older files.
		- 20261005: A run with the module cached takes 0.34 s, 0.33 s of CPU and 47 MB. What is left is `_initialize`, which upstream's lazier registry cuts. With both, 0.02 s and 39 MB. See 2026100519164742.
		- 20261005: Verified on vm925w: 1.8 s for the first run, 0.37 s after, with the file in `%LOCALAPPDATA%\zuid`. A byte flipped in it meant one slower run and a rewritten file. Verified on b26: the full pipeline, with the cache tests reading `~/Library/Caches/zuid`. The arm64 Windows build under Wine wrote its file too.
	- Decisions:
		- 20261005: the command only. The C module does not write into a caller's home directory, and a C caller makes one context and keeps it.
		- 20261005: skipped when run as root, since under sudo HOME can be a directory another user writes.
		- 20261005: `$XDG_CACHE_HOME/zuid`, else `~/.cache/zuid`; `~/Library/Caches/zuid` on macOS; `%LOCALAPPDATA%\zuid` on Windows. Nothing turns it off. Deleting it is safe.
	- Branch: fastcli
	- Test case: ddj72, ddj73, ddj74 and ddj75 in `cli-test.bash`, and ddj76 in `tests.zig`. Each failed with its part of the cache broken.
	- Acceptance signoff: Self-closed: tested on 3 OSes, uses each one's usual cache dir, and a bad file only costs one slower run.
	- Closed: 20261013-033301

- `libzuid.a`'s compiler_rt object can differ between 2 builds of one commit.
	- ID: 2026101305595488
	- Type: Bug
	- Status: Done
	- Needs local test suite run?: Done, 20261013. See Verified.
	- Needs external testing: Done on b26, 20261013.
	- Priority|Severity [Bug]: Avg
	- Opened: 20261013-055954
	- Opened by: JC
	- Parent ID: 2026101304122319
	- Target OS: Any
	- Steps to reproduce [Bug]:
		- Build a static library with `-fcompiler-rt` a few times, each with its own empty global cache, and compare the symbol names in `compiler_rt.o`.
	- Incorrect behavior [Bug]: Names such as `__anon_1006637151` against `__anon_10187`, and the sections named after them, so the size and every offset after them move.
	- Expected behavior [Bug]: The same bytes from any machine that builds the same commit.
	- Reproduced [Bug]: Yes, 20261013, with Zig 0.17.0 on b23. 9 cold builds with 8 to 32 threads gave 2 sets of names, and runs with the same flags disagreed, with ASLR on or off. 2 builds with `-j1` matched.
	- Possible cause [Bug]: The number is an index into the compiler's intern pool, and its top bits are the thread that added the value, so it depends on which thread got there first. No upstream issue found for that. Closest: ziglang/zig#36946, link inputs queued in a different order between runs, and #36334, debug info that changes with ASLR. #24116 asks for these names not to depend on the compiler's insides.
	- Actual cause [Bug]:
		- Confirmed. With `bundle_compiler_rt`, Zig builds a static library's compiler_rt as a second compile inside the first, on whatever worker thread is free. A value interned there has that thread's number in the top bits of its index, and the LLVM backend names it `__anon_<index>`. The main thread's number is 0.
		- Built as the root of its own compile step, compiler_rt is analyzed on the main thread, and its names come out the same on every run.
	- Progress log:
		- 20261013: Ruled out: `zig build -j` limits steps only and doesn't reach the compiler, and build.zig has no switch for the compiler's threads. Renaming the symbols after the build is a hack. It shows only with a cold cache, so a second build on one machine doesn't catch it.
		- 20261013: zuid's own object matched between b23 and the container. Only compiler_rt differed.
		- 20261013: Reproduced with a one-line library: 5 cold builds gave 5 sets of names, and 3 with `zig build-lib -j1` matched.
		- 20261013: Ruled out more switches. `zig build-lib -j<N>` exists, but the build system's compile step never passes it, and the compiler reads no environment variable for its thread count. Pinning the whole build to one CPU would slow every step. Building an object instead of a library puts compiler_rt in zuid's own compile, which is Zig's way for objects, but then compiler_rt gets zuid's optimize mode and safety checks, so its code changes.
		- 20261013: No upstream issue covers the thread number in these names. Codeberg has nothing closer than #36946 and #36334, above.
		- 20261013: Cold builds on b23 for the Linux release target took about 160 s on `main` and 146 to 163 s with the fix, with the machine busy. The new step takes about 3 s cold, beside the other steps.
	- Actual fix [Bug]:
		- `build.zig` builds compiler_rt as its own object step, with the settings Zig uses for its own copy, and adds it to the static library in place of `bundle_compiler_rt`.
	- Branch: crtrt
	- Commit: 57160c2
	- Test case: ddx4y.
		- It fails when any `__anon_N` in the built `libzuid.a` is 2^20 or more, which takes a worker thread's number. It failed before the fix with 17 of 392 names, and passes after. It skips when there are no such names, in case Zig names them another way later.
		- It can't see a difference that only 2 cold builds show. See Verified.
	- Swept: only `libzuid.a` keeps compiler_rt as an object. The command, `libzuid.so` and the Windows DLL link Zig's own compiler_rt, and the names are gone after the link: stripped, each matched across 5 cold builds. The Windows, arm64 Linux and FreeBSD static libraries build with the new object, and none has a thread number in its names. The archive has only compiler_rt and zuid's own object.
	- Verified:
		- `cicd.bash --quick --only zig` passed on b23 with the fix, 271 ok, ddx4y and the static library smoke tests included. Without the fix ddx4y failed.
		- 3 cold builds with the fix gave the same `compiler_rt.o` bytes. 2 on `main` gave 2 different ones.
		- A release build's `compiler_rt.o` has the same code, sections, relocations and symbols as Zig's own, apart from the names, for Linux x86_64 and arm64, FreeBSD, Windows x86_64 and arm64, and macOS x86_64 and arm64. A Debug build's `__clear_cache` lacks Zig's valgrind hint on Linux, FreeBSD and Windows x86_64.
		- 20261013: 2 cold `cicd.bash --package` runs on b23, the first a full run, gave the same `libzuid.a` for all 5 targets. A `--container --package` run gave the same 5 again. Every release file matched between b23 and the container apart from the Windows exe and dll, which are 2026101305595483.
		- 20261013: `cicd.bash --quick --package` passed on b26, ddx4y and ddwmp included.
	- Acceptance signoff: Self-closed 20261013. The fix does what the item asked, and its test failed before it and passes after.
	- Closed: 20261013-151453
	- Note: 20261013, the debug info in every file built here still differs between 2 cold builds. `tools/to_file.zig` is built in Debug and its bytes differ from one cold build to the next. The translate-c steps hash those bytes, so the translated headers go under new cache paths, and the paths are in the DWARF. Stripped files match. Filed as 2026101313551112.

- Debug info differs between 2 cold builds of one commit.
	- ID: 2026101313551112
	- Type: Bug
	- Status: Done
	- Needs local test suite run?: Done, 20261013. See Verified.
	- Needs external testing: Done on b26, 20261013.
	- Priority|Severity [Bug]: Avg
	- Opened: 20261013-135511
	- Opened by: JC
	- Related IDs: 2026101304122319, 2026101305595488
	- Target OS: Any
	- Steps to reproduce [Bug]:
		- Build the release twice, each with its own empty global cache, and compare the files in `dist/debug/`.
	- Incorrect behavior [Bug]: The debug info differs. The stripped files match.
	- Expected behavior [Bug]: The same bytes from any machine that builds the same commit.
	- Reproduced [Bug]: Yes, 20261013, on b23, while working 2026101305595488.
	- Possible cause [Bug]: `tools/to_file.zig` is built in Debug, and its bytes differ from one cold build to the next. The translate-c steps hash those bytes, so the translated headers go under new cache paths, and those paths end up in the DWARF.
	- Actual cause [Bug]:
		- Confirmed. Same class as 2026101305595488. `to_file` was built by Zig's own x86_64 backend, which compiles functions on many threads. Its code came out the same every time, but its symbol names and debug info did not: `__anon_N` names include the compiler's thread number, and the debug info is written in the order the threads finish.
		- A translate-c step hashes the program it runs, so each cold build put the translated headers in a new `o/<hash>` folder. Those folders are in the debug info of the command, `libzuid.so` and `libzuid.a`.
	- Progress log:
		- 20261013: 3 cold builds of `to_file` alone gave 3 different files. `.text` and the data matched; `.strtab`, `.debug_info`, `.debug_str` and `.debug_line` did not.
		- 20261013: Ruled out stripping alone. Zig's own backend keeps a symbol table with the `__anon_N` names, and 6 cold builds at `-j1` to `-j16` gave 6 files.
		- 20261013: Ruled out LLVM alone. Cold builds matched at the default thread count, but `-j1` gave other `__anon_N` numbers, so a builder with 1 CPU would differ.
		- 20261013: LLVM and stripped matched at `-j1`, `-j2` and the default. The Mac builds, cross-built from here, have no `__anon_N` names either. A cold build of it takes about 3 s either way.
		- 20261013: No upstream issue found for the thread order in Zig's own backend. Closest are the ones on 2026101305595488.
	- Actual fix [Bug]:
		- `build.zig` builds `to_file` with LLVM, stripped. Nothing reads its debug info.
	- Branch: tofile
	- Commit: 12cd4a6
	- Test case: ddx5r.
		- The Zig stage already builds into 2 new caches for dcrga, so each builds its own `to_file`. The check fails when the 2 differ, or when `to_file` has `__anon_` names, which a 1-CPU builder would number differently.
		- It failed both ways before the fix: the old `build.zig` gave 2 different files, and LLVM without strip kept the names. It passes after.
		- A full cold build compare takes about 5 minutes for one target, too slow for the pipeline. See Verified.
	- Swept: the only other Run steps are the test run, whose output nothing reads, and the macOS relink, a system command whose hash takes its arguments and inputs, not the program's bytes. compiler_rt is 2026101305595488.
	- Verified:
		- 2 cold release builds for `x86_64-linux-gnu.2.28` at one path, each with its own empty caches. Before the fix, the command, `libzuid.so` and `libzuid.a` differed, and only in the translated header folders. With those folder names swapped back, the command and `libzuid.so` matched byte for byte. After the fix all 3 matched, debug info included.
		- `cicd.bash --quick --only zig` passed with the fix, 272 ok and 3 skips, ddx5r and ddx4y included.
		- 20261013: 2 cold `cicd.bash --package` runs on b23 with the same cache paths, each starting empty. Every file in `dist/debug/` matched for Linux, FreeBSD and both Windows `libzuid.a` files. Only the Windows PDBs differed, which is 2026101305595483. With 2 different cache paths the translated header folders differ too, as expected, since their hash takes the path of `to_file`.
		- 20261013: `cicd.bash --quick --package` passed on b26, ddx5r included.
	- Acceptance signoff: Self-closed 20261013. The cause was found and fixed, and the check failed before the fix.
	- Closed: 20261013-151453

- Release files still differ between two machines building one commit.
	- ID: 2026101304122319
	- Type: Bug
	- Status: Done
	- Needs external testing: Done. b26, 20261013, see Verified.
	- Priority|Severity [Bug]: Avg
	- Opened: 20261013-041223
	- Opened by: JC
	- Related IDs: 2026101008194330, 2026101305595483, 2026101305595488
	- Target OS: Any
	- Steps to reproduce [Bug]:
		- Run `cicd.bash --package`, keep `dist/`, run `cicd.bash --container --package`, and compare the two.
	- Incorrect behavior [Bug]: The bare Linux and FreeBSD binaries match, and these differ:
		- The Windows `zuid.exe` and `zuid.dll`, in 20 bytes: the time stamp and the PDB's ID. The linker makes both from a hash, which takes in the PDB, and the PDB has the build machine's paths.
		- `libzuid.a`, in its compiler_rt object. Anonymous symbols and sections are numbered differently, such as `__anon_603986435` against `__anon_905974935`.
		- The tarballs, zips and packages, in file times, owners and modes.
	- Expected behavior [Bug]: The same bytes from any machine that builds the same commit.
	- Reproduced [Bug]: Yes, 20261013, b23 against the container, on branch `strip`.
	- Actual cause [Bug]:
		- Confirmed. Each file in a tarball or zip took its time from when it was copied, its owner from whoever built it, its mode from the umask, and its place from the directory order. nfpm stamped the deb and rpm with the clock, and the rpm with the build host.
		- The build number could be stale. Zig 0.17 reuses what build.zig worked out last time until build.zig or a `-D` option changes, and it doesn't watch the environment or git. So a new commit or SOURCE_DATE_EPOCH went unseen. The b23 release built from `main` had the strip merge's build number, 38 minutes older than its HEAD.
		- build.zig dropped SOURCE_DATE_EPOCH for translate-c by giving that step its own copy of the environment, and the copy went into the step's cache key. Once the build number was worked out on every run, any change to the environment rebuilt everything, with new cache paths in the debug info and the Windows PDBs.
		- The Windows exe and dll, and the compiler_rt object in every `libzuid.a`, come out of Zig itself. Split off as 2026101305595483 and 2026101305595488.
		- The deb and rpm also differ when nfpm's version does. b23 has 2.43.0 and the container pins 2.47.0. With 2.47.0 on both they match.
		- On a Mac, 3 more. Apple's `strip` rewrites `libzuid.a`'s symbol table with the clock's date and a mode from the umask. `dsymutil` writes the path it was given into the `.dSYM`'s relocation maps, and that path was in the run's temp dir. And a Mac gives symlinks a mode of their own, from the umask, so the dylib's soname links differed under umask 077.
	- Progress log:
		- 20261013: 2 package runs on b23, the second under umask 077, gave 10 different files of 33: every tarball, zip, deb and rpm, and the checksums.
		- 20261013: After the fix, b23 against the container: the bare Linux x86_64, arm64 and FreeBSD binaries and the FreeBSD tarball match. The Linux tarballs differ only in `libzuid.a`'s compiler_rt object. The Windows zips differ only there and in `zuid.exe` and `zuid.dll`, 20 bytes each. The deb and rpm match with the same nfpm. Tar and zip headers match.
		- 20261013: b23's nfpm was left at 2.43.0, since it's installed outside the project.
		- 20261013: b23's nfpm moved to the container's 2.47.0, from the same release file and hash. ddwx8 says so when a host's nfpm isn't the pinned one.
		- 20261013: Reproduced on b26 by packaging twice, the second under umask 077. The release file was the tarball, and in it only `libzuid.a` and the 2 dylib symlinks differed.
	- Actual fix [Bug]:
		- `package.bash` gives every file in a tarball, zip, deb and rpm one time: SOURCE_DATE_EPOCH, else the HEAD commit date, never the clock, and no earlier than 1980. Modes are 0755 or 0644 by whether the file runs. Tarballs are owner 0 by number with no names, sorted bytewise, and gzip keeps no name or time. Zips get the same list, with the zone pinned to UTC and no extra fields. nfpm gets the time, a fixed rpm build host and set modes on the license files.
		- `build.zig` tells Zig not to reuse its answers unless `-Dbuild-epoch` is given. That costs about 40 ms on a build with nothing to do.
		- `tools/to_file.zig` drops SOURCE_DATE_EPOCH for translate-c, so the step no longer keeps a copy of the environment.
		- On a Mac, `package.bash` runs `strip` on an archive with `ZERO_AR_DATE=1`, runs `dsymutil` from the slice on a relative path, and sets symlinks to 0755.
	- Note: 20261013, ddwmp failed on b26 at 2fa8524: 6 of 17 files differ under umask 077. 4 are the `.dSYM` relocation maps, `Relocations/<cpu>/*.yml`, and one is `checksums.txt`, so a release file differs too. The output named only 5.
	- Branch: repro, macfix
	- Commit: 0483497, 119c277
	- Test case: ddwmp and ddwn8.
		- ddwmp packages again after the first run, under umask 077 and, where a user namespace is allowed, as root on another host name. Every file has to match, debug info included. It failed before the fix with 10 of 33 files different, and with only the build.zig half of the fix with 23. About 24 s on b23 with a warm Zig cache.
		- It names every file that differs now. On b26 it failed before the Mac fix naming all 6, and passes after.
		- ddwn8 builds again in a cache that has seen another epoch, with SOURCE_DATE_EPOCH=1700000000, and wants that build number. It failed before the fix with the old one.
		- Neither catches a path or Zig's own output that differs between machines. The 2 split items have no check here for that reason.
		- ddwx8 skips with a note when the host's nfpm isn't the one `cicd/container/Dockerfile` pins. It skipped on b23's old one, which reports its version as "dev".
	- Swept: every `tar`, `zip` and nfpm call under `cicd/`. Only `package.bash` makes release files; `installer-test.bash` and `pipeline-test.bash` make their own fixtures. `build.zig` has no other step with its own environment. The macOS release goes through the same functions.
		- Mac: `strip` on an archive and `dsymutil` are each called once, in `fStripSlice`. The other archives on a Mac come from Zig, which writes date 0, and `lipo`. The only symlinks are the dylib's soname links, made by `cp -P` in `fMergeSlices`. Linux symlinks have no mode to set.
	- Verified: `cicd.bash --quick --only zig --package` passed on b23 and with `--container`, 20261013, ddwmp and ddwn8 included. The comparison above is from those 2 runs, with a third host package run using nfpm 2.47.0 for the deb and rpm.
		- `cicd.bash --quick --only zig --package` passed on b26 at 119c277, 20261013, 276 ok, ddwmp included. On b23, `cicd.bash --quick` passed, and so did `--quick --only zig --package`, ddwmp included.
	- Acceptance signoff: Self-closed: reproduced, ddwmp failed before the fix and passes after on b26. The Windows and compiler_rt parts are 2026101305595483 and 2026101305595488.
	- Closed: 20261013-130845

- `dcrga` fails on a new Zig install or with Zig's cache cleared.

	- Type: Bug
	- Status: Done
	- Priority|Severity [Bug]: Avg
	- Opened: 20261010-081943
	- Opened by: JC
	- Related IDs: 2026100415475381, 2026101008194270
	- Target OS: Any
	- Steps to reproduce [Bug]:
		- Run `cicd.bash` with nothing in Zig's global cache for the Zig in use, such as a newly built container image.
	- Incorrect behavior [Bug]: `dcrga` fails. Zig's debug libunwind build gets the empty `SOURCE_DATE_EPOCH`, and clang refuses it.

	- Opened by: JC
	- Related IDs: 2026100318410002, 2026100313583935, 2026093018112445
	- Target OS: Any
	- Steps to reproduce [Bug]:
		- In `zig/`: `SOURCE_DATE_EPOCH= ZIG_GLOBAL_CACHE_DIR=<new dir> zig build --cache-dir <new dir>`, with Zig 0.17.0.
	- Incorrect behavior [Bug]: The build fails. Zig's own libunwind build passes the empty value to clang, which refuses it.
	- Expected behavior [Bug]: The build finishes and takes the commit date, as it does with a warm global cache.
	- Reproduced [Bug]: Yes, 20261004 on Linux, on `main` and on branch `machang`.
	- Possible cause [Bug]: Since 0.17, `build.zig` cannot clear the variable for the compile steps, only for Run steps. `dcrga` misses it, since it gives the build a fresh local cache but keeps the warm global one.
	- Actual cause [Bug]: Upstream behavior, not this repo. Zig 0.17.0 runs clang with its own environment when it builds libunwind and libc into the global cache, and clang refuses an empty or non-numeric value. A cached copy never runs clang, and the value is not part of the cache key. zuid has no C sources of its own, so these are the only places clang runs.
	- Progress log:
		- 20261004: Reproduced on Linux: the build failed on `UnwindRegistersSave.S` and `UnwindRegistersRestore.S`, plus clang notes from the C files with no file name. With nothing set, the same cold build took 2 min 28 s. An empty program linking libc and libunwind fails the same way, in 4 s.
		- 20261004: Read in 0.17.0's `src/Compilation.zig`: `evalZigLlvmProcess` spawns `zig clang` with no environment of its own, and the Maker spawns compile steps with its own environment too. Outside Debug, Zig passes `-Werror=date-time`, so the value cannot change those libraries.
		- 20261004: No report found on Codeberg or the old GitHub tracker. Draft, not filed: "Zig's own libunwind and libc builds fail with an empty SOURCE_DATE_EPOCH on a cold global cache. `SOURCE_DATE_EPOCH= zig build-exe a.zig -lc -lunwind` with a new `ZIG_GLOBAL_CACHE_DIR` fails with clang's 'must be a non-negative decimal integer' error; with a warm cache it works. Whether a build passes depends on cache state. The variable is not in those libraries' cache keys, and `-Werror=date-time` keeps it from changing them outside Debug. Suggest not passing it to these sub-builds, or dropping an invalid one."
		- 20261004: `dcrga` stays on the warm global cache. A direct `zig build` with the value empty fails cold whatever this repo does, so a cold `dcrga` could never pass. A cold run through the scripts would add about 2.5 min, mostly rebuilding Zig's build runner; Zig's own libc, libunwind and compiler_rt take about 8 s of that.
	- Actual fix [Bug]: `cicd.bash`, in preflight, and `package.bash` drop an empty or non-numeric value before running zig, with a note for a non-numeric one, the same as `build.zig` treats it. The README says to unset it for a plain `zig build`.
	- Verified: a full `cicd.bash --no-dogfood --no-backup -q` run with the value empty and a new global cache passed, in 6 min 13 s. The same in a clone of `main`, with `--only zig --quick`, failed in the first Zig build with clang's error.
	- Note: 20261004, on b26 at 12d2b2d: the full `--package` run passed, with `ddgmn`, `ddgmp` and `dcrga`. The variable was not set there, so that run did not try an empty one on a cold global cache.
	- Swept: every `zig` call in `cicd.bash` runs after preflight; `package.bash` is the other script that runs zig, and the harnesses run only stand-in zigs. `install.bash` and `install.ps1` do not build.
	- Branch: emptyepoch
	- Commit: e8f7545
	- Test case: new `ddgmn` and `ddgmp` in `pipeline-test.bash` check that `cicd.bash` and `package.bash` keep an empty, blank or non-numeric value from zig, and keep a number. Both failed on the old scripts and pass now.
	- Acceptance signoff: Self-closed: the pipeline builds with the value empty, and the cause is upstream.
	- Closed: 20261004-184405

- `pipeline-test.bash` fails on a Mac with the stock Python.
	- ID: 2026100417561172
	- Type: Bug
	- Status: Done
	- Needs external testing: No. `pipeline-test.bash` passed on b26 with the fix, and the rest of the full run passed there with the fixture written the same way.
	- Priority|Severity [Bug]: Avg
	- Opened: 20261004-175611
	- Opened by: JC
	- Target OS: macOS
	- Test environment: b26, macOS 15.8, Python 3.9.6, the only `python3` there.
	- Version and build: 12d2b2d
	- Steps to reproduce [Bug]:
		- On b26, run `cicd.bash --no-sync --no-backup --no-dogfood -q`.
	- Incorrect behavior [Bug]: The shell stage stops in `pipeline-test.bash`, after the `--publish` cases. `test-ids.py new --at 2026-09-01T12:00:00Z` throws "Invalid isoformat string", and the next line dies on an unset `fixtureIds[2]`. Nothing past the shell stage runs.
	- Expected behavior [Bug]: The shell stage passes, as it did on b26 before the fixture came in.
	- Reproduced [Bug]: Yes, on b26, 20261004.
	- Possible cause [Bug]: Python before 3.11 cannot read a trailing `Z` in `fromisoformat`. The fixture came in with 34a071b. Not yet decided whether the fix goes in the fixture or in `test-ids.py`.
	- Progress log:
		- 20261004: With that one fixture date written as `+00:00`, the rest of the full `--package` run passed on b26.
		- 20261004: Every Python script the pipeline runs was checked against 3.9. Only `--at` with a `Z` needed newer. `gen-demo-gif.py` needs 3.11 for `tomllib` and already says so in one line, and the demo stage skips when it fails.
		- 20261004: A bad `--at` threw a traceback too. It now prints one line and exits 2.
		- 20261004: README lists Python 3.9 for the pipeline and 3.11 for the demo.
	- Actual cause [Bug]:
		- `test-ids.py new --at` handed the time straight to `fromisoformat`, which takes a trailing `Z` only from Python 3.11. The script header promises an ISO 8601 time, and `Z` is ISO.
	- Decisions:
		- The fix goes in `test-ids.py`, not the fixture, since its header promises ISO times.
		- Python 3.9 is the floor for the pipeline, since that is what stock macOS has. `gen-demo-gif.py` is the one exception, at 3.11, and the demo stage is optional.
	- Actual fix [Bug]: `--at` turns a trailing `Z` into `+00:00` before parsing. The fixture now uses `+00:00`, so a `Z` failure shows as its own test instead of killing the run.
	- Verified: On b26 with Python 3.9.6, `pipeline-test.bash` failed `ddgqv` and `ddgqw` with the old `test-ids.py`, and passed all 48 with the fix. Here on 3.13 it passes all 48, and `ddgqw` fails with the old script. `test-ids.py check` and shellcheck pass.
	- Swept: `flame-report.py`, `pprof2flame.py`, `gen-demo-gif.py` and `test-ids.py`, for `fromisoformat`, `match`, `X | Y` unions, `zip(strict=)`, `datetime.UTC`, `tomllib`, parenthesized `with`, `pairwise` and `bit_count`. All four parse and start on b26's 3.9.6. The inline `python3 -c` lines in `cicd.bash`, `cli-test.bash` and `installer-test.bash` use nothing past 3.9.
	- Branch: pyfloor
	- Commit: 99f35d1
	- Test case: `ddgqv` (`--at` with `Z` matches `+00:00`) and `ddgqw` (a bad `--at` gives one line, no traceback), in `pipeline-test.bash`. `ddgqv` passes on 3.11 and newer either way; only a 3.9 or 3.10 run proves it.
	- Acceptance signoff: Self-closed: the test failed before the fix and passes after, on the Mac that found it.
	- Closed: 20261004-181500

- A clean build failed with `SOURCE_DATE_EPOCH` set but empty.
	- ID: 2026093018112445
	- Type: Bug
	- Status: Done
	- Priority|Severity [Bug]: Avg
	- Opened: 20260930-181124
	- Opened by: JC
	- Target OS: Any
	- Steps to reproduce [Bug]:
		- `SOURCE_DATE_EPOCH= zig build` with an empty cache.
	- Incorrect behavior [Bug]: Clang refuses the empty value and every `@cImport` fails.
	- Expected behavior [Bug]: The build falls back to the commit date, which `build.zig` already meant to do.
	- Reproduced [Bug]: Yes, on Linux and macOS.
	- Actual cause [Bug]:
		- `build.zig` ignored the value for its own stamp but left it set for the compiler. A warm cache never runs clang, so `dcrga` passed anyway.

	- Test case: `dcrga`, now on its own cache. Watched to fail without the fix.
	- Closed: 20260930-181124

- `install.ps1` installed without asking when nothing answered its prompt.
	- ID: 2026093015570558
	- Type: Bug
	- Status: Done
	- Priority|Severity [Bug]: Avg
	- Opened: 20260930-155200
	- Opened by: JC
	- Target OS: Any, under pwsh
	- Steps to reproduce [Bug]:
		- Run `install.ps1` without `-Yes`, with stdin at its end, such as from a scheduled task or `< /dev/null`.
	- Incorrect behavior [Bug]: It prints the plan, then downloads and installs.
	- Expected behavior [Bug]: No answer means no, as `[y/N]` says. `install.bash` already stops there.
	- Reproduced [Bug]: Yes, by the new no-answer case in `installer-test.bash`.
	- Actual cause [Bug]:
		- `Read-Host` gives back nothing at the end of stdin, and nothing `-notmatch` a pattern is not true. So the "no" branch never ran.

	- Commit: f4c69e3
	- Test case: `ddb12`, `ddb14`.
	- Closed: 20260930-155553

A Mac that doesn't answer, stays taken or can't be woken hands the job to the next, with a note saying why. A job that fails is a fail, and isn't tried again on the next. When every Mac skips, the step is one `SKIP` with each one's reason. The step's title names both, "Mac box: b26 or vmKt6m".

Zig 0.17.0 went in from its release tarball as `~/.local/zig-x86_64-macos-0.17.0`, as on b26, and PowerShell 7.6.6 as `~/.local/bin/pwsh`, which the Mac's own pipeline tests need for deea7. Both checked against their published sha256. No Homebrew build was needed.

The box's work folder was removed after.
		- 20261026: `pipeline-test.bash` 166 of 166, shellcheck on `cicd.bash` and `pipeline-test.bash`, and `cicd.bash --backlog-run-quick shell docs`, 8 of 8 steps.

	- Acceptance signoff: Self-closed 20261026. Pipeline-only, and run on the real box.

- Identifiers from one tick have no counter.
	- ID: 2026102409130649
	- Type: Feature
	- Status: Done
		- Proposals are accepted.
	- Needs local test suite run?: none.
	- Priority [Feature|Enhancement] | Severity [Bug]: Avg
	- Opened: 20261024-091306
	- Opened by: JC
	- Related IDs: 2026101410495988
	- Requirements  [Feature]:
		- A counter component, so identifiers from one tick sort in the order they were made.
	- Progress log:
		- 20261024: From the README review. Today 2 identifiers from one tick sort in random order with `%r`, and repeat without it. design.md keeps same-tick repeats literal with a warning, and a counter is a way to ask for something else in the format.
		- 20261024: Question, with a proposal for each:
			- Name: `%c`.
			- Scope: one run of the command, or one Go `Generator` or C context. Nothing is shared between processes, since that needs a state file, and there's no config file for the same reason. 2 processes in one tick still need `%h`, `%u` or `%r` to differ.
			- Reset: back to 0 when the tick changes. A run reads the clock once, so in a batch it's the line number from 0.
			- Width: fixed and derived from a target, like the other components, so identifiers still split by offset. 20 bits, which is 4 symbols in base 62. Past it is an error, as `%d` is past the horizon.
			- The C context keeps the last tick and the count. That changes "the context keeps only the runtime and the clock pin", since a count can't come in per call.
			- A format with `%c` can't repeat within a run, so the repeat warning never fires for it.
		- 20261025: Done in Go, the Zig core, the C context, the command, the vectors, errors.tsv, zuid.h, design.md and the README. 11 vectors, from count 0 to a carry, in 4 bases and at millisecond precision. The `count=` env key replays that many identifiers from one counter first.
		- 20261025: dee9g in `pipeline-test.bash` failed every run, since its list of `--short` cases still had 10 without deec4. deec4 is in the list now, so the short run stays at 11.
		- 20261025: The Go soak, de5cg, failed on `counter-last-digit`, since it replayed the vectors without `count=` and kept the count from the row before. It and daq6x now replay each row through one shared helper. The Zig replayer already read `count=`, and the C soak and the fuzzers don't replay the vectors.
	- Decisions:
		- 20261024: The proposal above, as asked. Its C context change goes in design.md beside the old rule.
		- 20261025: A full tick is its own error on every surface: C code 15 `ZUID_ERR_COUNTER`, Go `ErrCounter`, Zig `CounterFull`. Code 12 says the clock is past the horizon, which isn't what went wrong.
		- 20261025: The limit is 2^20 counts in every base, the way the horizon is the same in every base, even where the width holds more.
		- 20261025: A new precision starts the count over, the same as a new tick. So does a clock that steps back.
		- 20261025: One number per identifier, however many times `%c` appears in it. A format without `%c` spends none.
		- 20261025: An identifier that fails gives its number back, so a C caller retrying with a bigger buffer, or the command growing its own, gets the same one. In Go only the latest number can come back, so a failure among goroutines can leave a gap. Gaps cost nothing.
		- 20261025: Go reads the clock once per identifier now, so `%d` and `%c` agree on the tick. It read it once per `%d` before.
		- 20261025: A core call with no counter, such as a lone identifier, renders `%c` as 0.
		- 20261025: The repeat warning still suggests `%r` only, since dcrp0 pins its text and the issue didn't ask to change it.
	- Branch: round1026
	- Test case: deebw and deebx (core width and counter), deeby, deebz and deec0 (Go), deec1 (C context), deec2, deec3 and deec4 (command), the 11 `counter-*` rows in daq6x and daqq6, and the `counter-full` row in ddj77, ddj8d, ddj8e, ddj8f and ddj8g. deeby, deebx and deec1 failed with the give-back taken out, and deeby with the tick reset taken out.
	- Verified:
		- 20261025: `go test ./...`, `-race` over the counter, vector and error tests, `zig build test`, `cli-test.bash` 97 of 97 and `--short` 11 of 11, `test-ids.py check`, shellcheck, gofmt, zig fmt, the scrub and the markdown checks.
		- 20261025: dee9g failed before its fix and passed after. `pipeline-test.bash` 165 of 165, `cli-test.bash --short` 11 of 11, shellcheck on the stage's list, `test-ids.py check` and the scrub.
		- 20261025: de5cg failed before its fix and passed after, run alone with `ZUID_SOAK_DIR` set, as the leak check runs it. `go test ./...`, `go vet`, gofmt, `test-ids.py check` and the scrub.
		- Not verified: the full run.
	- Swept: every verb switch and verb list (`spentBy` in Go, the core loop, `unknownVerb`, both unknown-component messages), the help, zuid.h's component list and context note, design.md's component table, width rule, refusals and C context rule, and the Go fuzz property.
	- Closed: 20261025-203628
	- 20261025: Verified in the full run log of 20261025-200553_004 at da19f12: dcrh6, dcrh7 and dcrh8 in the fuzz stage, deebz and de54e under -race, the C checks and smoke tests against the new header, and de5cg (the Go soak) all PASS.

- Run CICD on GitHub's free arm64 macOS runners.
	- ID: 2026102318361185
	- Type: Enhancement
	- Status: Done
	- Needs local test suite run?: none.
	- Needs external testing: none. the `macos` workflow on `dev` at d5b6fe9, run 40233761521, a full run on the arm64 runner, passed all 22 steps, 527 PASS and 0 FAIL, with Go and Zig fuzz, the Go soak and the profile, and the docker checks skipping.
	- Priority [Feature|Enhancement] | Severity [Bug]: Avg
	- Opened: 20261023-183527
	- Opened by: JC
	- Related IDs: 2026101312344407
	- Target OS: macOS
	- Requirements  [Feature]:
		- Build, run the tests, and smoke-test the CLI on the built binary.
		- Sweeping override, 2026-10-24: this supersedes any earlier decision on this project that conflicts with it.
	- Progress log:
		- 20261023: It would be the first run of the arm64 slice on Apple Silicon, which 2026101312344407 is stalled on.
		- 20261023: `.github/workflows/macos.yml` runs `cicd.bash --quick --package` on `macos-15`, then `cli-test.bash` on the universal release. It runs on a push to any branch, skipping docs-only pushes, or by hand.
		- 20261023: `--package` on a Mac now runs each slice it can, the x86_64 one under Rosetta on Apple Silicon, and links and runs `capi_smoke.c` against the tarball, static and shared, per slice. An Intel Mac links the arm64 one and skips running it.
		- 20261023: The runner needed the Dockerfile's exact Go. setup-go turns off toolchain downloads, and the release check refuses any other Go for the reactor.
		- 20261023: Found there: ddwj4 failed on `/Users/runner` in the release. Wasmtime is built on GitHub's runners, and its build paths stay in every binary that links it, so a runner's home matches them. When a vendored Wasmtime archive already has the home in it, only the checkout and Zig paths are looked for now. The run before the fix failed on it, and the one after passed.
		- 20261023: Waits on signoff for the trigger: a push to any branch, docs-only pushes skipped. The arm64 macOS half of 2026101312344407 ran in this run.
		- 20261024: Answered: use GitHub's free arm64 macOS runner, and other boxes, but not with `--quick`. So the workflow does the full run, less what needs docker.
		- 20261024: The CICD reset's `macOS builds on GitHub` matches what's here. What's left is the answer above, and picking which flags start the workflow by how long they take, in place of every push to any branch.
		- 20261025: The workflow drops `--quick`. It runs the full run with `--no-demo` and `--no-remote`, and the docker checks skip by themselves.
		- 20261025: A full run, `--long`, `--backlog-run-full` and `--merge-gate` start it, as a step right after the sync, and a step after the checks waits for it. `--quick`, `--backlog-run-quick` and `--only` don't, and `--no-remote` turns it off.
		- 20261025: It runs only when the remote's branch is at HEAD, since GitHub runs what it has. A detached HEAD, no gh, an unreachable remote, a refused dispatch, a cancelled run or no answer in 90 minutes is a SKIP. Only a finished run that failed is a FAIL.
		- 20261025: The workflow starts by itself only on a push to main now, besides by hand. The hook's merge gate runs before main has the commit, so that push is what covers main.
		- 20261025: dedty's full `--list` now runs with `ZUID_IN_CONTAINER` unset, since the run in the image never plans the steps only the outside runs. Its checks are the same.
	- Verified:
		- 20261025: `pipeline-test.bash` 140 of 140, here and with `ZUID_IN_CONTAINER=1`. shellcheck, `test-ids.py check` (493, all unique), and `cicd.bash --backlog-run-quick shell docs`, 8 of 8 steps. `--list` shows both steps on a full run and `--backlog-run-full`, and `Off` for `--quick` and `--no-remote`. The `gh run list` and `gh run view` queries the result step makes answered as expected against this repo's real runs. Load about 5 on 32 CPUs.
		- Not verified: a real dispatch and wait, and the full run on the Mac.
		- 20261023, run 40059817674 on `macos-15` at 878f5b9: `cicd.bash -q --quick --package --no-sync --no-backup --no-dogfood` passed, 473 ok, no failures. `cli-test.bash` passed 89 on the universal release, the arm64 slice natively.
		- Locally: shellcheck, `test-ids.py check` (442, all unique), `pipeline-test.bash` 94 passed, the content scrub.
		- Not verified: the new checks were not watched to fail.
	- Decisions:
		- 20261023: `macos-15`, not M4. GitHub's free arm64 runners are M1 class, and M4 is not offered free. 15 is the free arm64 image closest to the release's macOS 13.0 floor.
		- 20261023: Zig comes from ziglang.org, checked against a pinned sha256 and cached between runs. No third-party setup action.
		- 20261023: Lint, the Go checkers and the leak checks stay on Linux. The runner has no docker, and shellcheck there would not match the pinned one.
		- 20261025: The full runs start it, since the hosted run takes about as long as they do. On a quick run it would double the wait.

		- 20261025: The guard is `cicd/utility/include/safe-rm.bash`: `fSafeRm` for a temp path, and `fSafeRm_Under` for one under a named parent. All 16 recursive removals in `cicd.bash` use it. The 4 Zig vendor ones name `zig/vendor`. GNU `rm` gets `--one-file-system`, BSD `rm` gets `-x`.
		- 20261025: `dev.run` and container names are the run folder's name now, such as `20261025-141812_001`.
		- 20261025: Found while testing: a SIGTERM or SIGHUP ran the cleanup, then the run went on, and exited 0. Now it stops with exit 1, like Ctrl+C.
		- 20261025: `cicd.bash` exits at once when the guard file is missing. A copy of the script without it ran on and left its folder, which ddhnc's copied tree showed. ddhnc copies the guard now.
