		- 20261025: The `macos` rerun (run 40243465349) then failed in the Go consumer step: the guard refused its scratch folder as a path through a link. macOS's bare `mktemp -d` ignores `TMPDIR` and uses its own temp folder, behind `/var`. All 11 in `cicd.bash` go through `fMkTempDir` in the guard's file now, which makes a `tmp.*` folder in `TMPDIR` by its physical path. The scratch cleanup reads `TMPDIR` the same way (90e1488).

Only these 2 named more than one. For the `mktemp` fix: every `mktemp` in `cicd.bash`, the harnesses, the hook, `package.bash`, the guard, the box scripts and `install.bash`. Only the 11 in `cicd.bash` had no template, and dee5j now checks them all.

	- Requirements  [Feature]:
		- `package.bash` removes its work folder through the guard, and empties `dist/` or `--out` through one that names that folder as its one parent.
		- `install.bash` and `install.ps1` carry their own guard, since they run alone from a download. The install dir is the one parent each names. `install.bash` stays Bash 3.2.
		- The 4 `rm -rf` in the 2 Dockerfiles run inside the image build, on fixed paths. Left as they are unless asked.
	- 20261025: the guard from 2026102514072140 is in at 5b2f7c3 and only its full run is left, so it no longer holds this back.
	- Progress log:
		- 20261025: `package.bash` works in a run folder from the guard's file, removed through `fSafeRm`. `dist/` or `--out` is emptied one entry at a time through `fSafeRm_Under`, with that folder, resolved, as the parent. A link in it is only unlinked.
		- 20261025: `install.bash` has its own `fSafeRm_Under`, Bash 3.2. The install dir is emptied one entry at a time with it as the parent, then filled or, on uninstall, `rmdir`ed. It has to be named `zuid` and not be a link. Its temp dir is `zuid-install.XXXXXX` in `TMPDIR`, removed through the same guard.
		- 20261025: `install.ps1` has `Remove-Under` and `Clear-InstallDirectory`, ASCII only. They walk the tree and unlink a link rather than follow it, which Windows PowerShell 5.1's `Remove-Item -Recurse` can't promise. Its temp dir goes the same way.
		- 20261025: Found while testing: the new temp dir's name starts with `zuid-`, and `install.bash` looked for the unpacked release as `zuid-*` from depth 0, so it took the temp dir itself. It looks from depth 1 now.
		- Verified: `installer-test.bash` 95 of 95, the bash 3.2 cases included, and in the image. PSScriptAnalyzer finds nothing in `install.ps1`.
		- Verified: Against the old scripts, dee2e, dee2f and dee2g failed. dee2h and dee2j pass either way here, since `rm -rf` and PowerShell 7 already don't follow a link. They're there for 5.1.
	- Decisions:
		- 20261025: An install dir reached through a link higher up, such as a linked home, is still allowed. It's resolved, and the resolved dir has to be named `zuid`. Only an install dir that is itself a link is refused.
		- 20261025: The Dockerfiles' `rm -rf` are left as they are.
	- Swept: dee2c checks every recursive removal in both installers and `package.bash`. The installers' other removals are a single file or link, with `rm -f` or `Remove-Item -Force`.

- Leak, memory, and correctness checks in CICD.
	- ID: 2026101816243853
	- Type: Enhancement
	- Status: Done
	- Priority [Feature|Enhancement] | Severity [Bug]: Avg
	- Opened: 20261018-162438
	- Opened by: JC
	- Related IDs: 2026101410495995
	- Requirements  [Feature]:
		- Only for code in use, that end users of this project use. That's the Go module, and the C API through its C tests. The Python scripts are pipeline tooling, and there's no Rust or C++ of ours.
		- For all code:
			- The checks cover first-party and third-party code. Vendored code is included.
				- On third-party code, run the leak, memory, and correctness checks named here, not style lints. Style findings in someone else's crate or module aren't ours to fix.
				- CodeQL reports only on source inside the database's root. For its run, vendor the dependencies into the tree first (`go mod vendor`).
			- Any report fails the test that made it.
			- Any leak is fixed. If the fix has to wait, it gets a backlog item, and the test that shows it turns the check off with that item's ID beside it.
			- A `--leakcheck` flag adds the slow checks:
				- CodeQL, from a pinned CLI run locally. No hosted service.
				- A soak test. It loops through the program's main actions headless, and fails if the heap at round 2N is larger than at round N by more than a set margin. The heap report is kept as a pipeline artifact.
			- Every tool is pinned and runs in the build container or lint image, never installed on the host.
			- Each new gate is watched to fail before it's trusted, by planting the leak it's meant to catch and seeing it go red.
			- Known-advisory scans run on every full run: `govulncheck`. They cover third-party code only, which is the point.
		- For Go:
			- Go leaks are goroutines that never exit, and references held too long: a map or slice that only grows, or a small slice keeping a big backing array alive.
			- Every package's tests run `goleak.VerifyTestMain(m)` from `go.uber.org/goleak`, pinned in go.mod. A goroutine still running after the tests fails the run, whether our code or a dependency started it.
			- Every full run runs the whole test suite with `-race`.
			- If the code uses cgo, every full run also runs the tests with `-asan`, in a build dir of its own.
			- Every full run runs `go vet` and `staticcheck`. `go vet`'s `lostcancel` and `copylocks` checks are the leak-related ones.
			- The soak test reads `runtime.MemStats.HeapInuse` and `runtime.NumGoroutine()` at round N and 2N, after `runtime.GC()`. Its heap profile (`pprof`) is kept as the artifact, and `go tool pprof -base` shows what grew.
			- Traps:
				- `goleak.VerifyNone` per test doesn't work with `t.Parallel()`. Use `VerifyTestMain`.
				- goleak ignores by function name. That's the only form of suppression allowed.
				- `go test` caches a pass. Run with `-count=1`.
				- One `runtime.GC()` can leave garbage behind. Call it twice before reading the stats, or the margin has to be wide enough to hide a real leak.
				- `-race` needs cgo on some platforms. A job with `CGO_ENABLED=0` fails to build it or skips it, so check the job's env.
				- `-race` is 2 to 20 times slower and uses 5 to 10 times the memory. Size the job's timeout for it.
		- For C:
			- Every full run builds the whole test suite with AddressSanitizer, LeakSanitizer, and UndefinedBehaviorSanitizer, in a build dir of its own.
				- Any report fails the test that made it.
				- Any leak is fixed. If the fix has to wait, it gets a backlog item, and the test that shows it turns leak detection off with that item's ID beside it.
				- For a full leak stack, rerun the test with `ASAN_OPTIONS=fast_unwind_on_malloc=0`.
			- Every full run also runs gcc's `-fanalyzer` and the clang static analyzer over every first-party .c file.
				- Before the gate goes live, every existing finding is fixed, or suppressed at the line with a one-line reason. There's no baseline file.
				- They move behind a flag only if a full run measures too slow.
			- A `--leakcheck` flag adds the slow checks:
				- CodeQL, from a pinned CLI run locally. No hosted service.
				- The clang analyzer in cross-file (CTU) mode, so it follows calls across .c files.
				- A heaptrack soak test. It loops through the program's main actions headless, and fails if the heap at round 2N is larger than at round N by more than a set margin. The heaptrack report is kept as a pipeline artifact.
			- Coverity Scan and Infer aren't used. Coverity uploads the build to a third party.
			- Every tool is pinned and runs in the build container or lint image, never installed on the host.
			- Each new gate is watched to fail before it's trusted: plant a leak, a null dereference, and a loop that grows the heap, and see each one go red.
			- Traps:
				- `-fanalyzer` does nothing under `-fsyntax-only`. Compile with `-c -o /dev/null`.
				- With `-Werror`, analyzer findings print as `error: ... [-Werror=analyzer-*]`, not `warning:`. A parser that only looks for warnings counts 0.
				- heaptrack and ASan can't share a build. The soak test uses a plain build.
				- ASan's allocator hides the heap from glibc's own stats, such as `mallinfo2`. A test that reads them should exit as skipped under ASan, not pass.
	- Progress log:
		- 20261018: Split into 2026101816474400 for Go, 2026101816474401 for C, and 2026101816474402 for `--leakcheck`. This one closes when they do.
		- 20261019: All 3 children are in and waiting on signoff.
	- Test case: the children's.
	- Acceptance signoff: Self-closed: with its 3 children.
	- Closed: 20261024-072500

		- 20261019: staticcheck and govulncheck are pinned in the container's Dockerfile, not in `fConfig` as 2026101410495995 asked, per the decision below.

With no docker that stage skips with a note, so a public clone still passes. Inside a `--container` run the tools are called directly. The Go toolchain's own `go vet` and `go test -race` may run on the host's Go.

		- 20261018: The module needs no cgo. If `-race` needs cgo here, it's handled per the parent's traps.
		- 20261018: Each gate is watched to fail on a planted fault before it's trusted.
		- 20261019: A Go library advisory in code we call fails the run. The Go that's scanned builds the reactor that ships in the command, so it has to stay current. The fix is a `GO_VER` bump, 2026101907263616.
	- Swept: every `go test` call in `cicd.bash`. The fuzz and benchmark runs pass with goleak in. The tests also compile for Windows and macOS with the new `TestMain`.

		- 20261019: The Go soak runs every vector, the refusals, and live identifiers from 4 goroutines through new generators, 80 rounds and then 80 more. The heap has to stay within 128 KB and the goroutine count can't grow. Both heap profiles are kept in `cicd/artifacts/leakcheck/`.
		- 20261019: The C soak has 2 parts, with an 8 KB margin. One context's calls run 100 rounds and 100 more under heaptrack, whose report is kept. Whole contexts run 8 and 8 more without it, since `zuid_new` compiles the module and heaptrack's own memory then grew about 70 MB a round. With glibc's per-thread cache off, both held to the byte.
		- 20261019: heaptrack's leak list names compile buffers that the heap reading shows freed, so it isn't a verdict.
		- 20261019: clang's analyzer runs in cross-file mode over the C tests. None of them calls into another, so a pair of files with a null dereference only that mode can see runs first, every time.
		- 20261019: Each gate failed first on a planted fault: a slice that only grows and a stuck goroutine in the Go module, a missing `zuid_free`, a lost buffer and a growing cache in copies of the C soak, and the cross-file pair with that mode off. A cache of one short string a round passed, being under the margin.

		- 20261019: CodeQL 2.27.2 runs from an image of its own, built on the main one, from GitHub's C and Go bundles pinned by sha256. It's about 480 MB more, so only `--leakcheck` builds or starts it. Under `--container --leakcheck` the whole run is in that image. It takes about 25 s a language.

		- 20261019: Go: the module is copied to a scratch folder and vendored there, so convertbase and shcl are under the database's root. So is the reactor, since the command runs it, and it's built for wasm the way the release builds it. Our code gets the whole security and quality suite, and third-party code only its security and correctness queries.
		- 20261019: C: every tracked .c file is compiled fresh while CodeQL watches. Zig isn't a language CodeQL reads, so the C module's own code isn't in the database, only its header through the C tests. There's no third-party C.
		- 20261019: Both fail if a file the build compiled isn't in the database. A C build that compiles nothing is refused by CodeQL itself. Go's extractor read every file even with a warm build cache, but the cache is fresh anyway.

- Non-release builds don't show their build date.
	- ID: 2026102318361186
	- Type: Enhancement
	- Status: Done
	- Needs external testing: Done. The `macos` workflow passed at 1942bb2, run 40063604025, with debmw and debmx in it.
	- Priority [Feature|Enhancement] | Severity [Bug]: Avg
	- Opened: 20261023-183527
	- Opened by: JC
	- Related IDs: 2026102321183047
	- Requirements  [Feature]:
		- For non-release builds, include " (YYYY-mm-DD)" after the build number.
			- Report the date as the local date of whoever views the version.
	- Progress log:
		- 20261023: The date has to be worked out when `--version` runs, from the minutes in the build number, since the build machine's time zone isn't the viewer's.
		- 20261023: Done. A non-release build prints `1.0.0-beta.1 (build debmp) (2026-10-23)`. The command takes the build number's minute and asks libc for the local date, so `TZ` and the system zone both count.
		- 20261023: ddb04's pattern now allows the date. Old: ends in `\(build [0-9a-z]+\)$`. New: the same, then an optional ` \([0-9]{4}-[0-9]{2}-[0-9]{2}\)`.
	- Decisions:
		- 20261023: A release build is one made with `-Drelease`, which only `package.bash` passes. The dogfood build and the release are both ReleaseSafe, and the tag doesn't exist yet when a release is packaged, so neither could tell them apart.
		- 20261023: The date is its own pair of parens after the build number's, `(build debmp) (2026-10-23)`, rather than inside it.
		- 20261023: `--about` and `--help` show the same line as `--version`. The Windows version resource stays without the date, since it's fixed at build time.
	- Verified: `--version` gave 2026-10-23 in PDT and at UTC-12, and 2026-10-24 at UTC and UTC+14, for a commit at 2026-10-24 04:06 UTC. A `-Drelease` build has no date, and its `--about` differs only in that. The x86_64 Windows build gave the same dates with `TZ` unset and at UTC+14. Windows x86_64 and arm64 and FreeBSD cross-build, and `clock.h` gives `localtime_r` for both macOS slices.
		- `cli-test.bash` passed 90 of 90 on both builds, and the old ddb04 failed on the dated one.

- Run on the last Core 2 Duo, and AMD CPUs from that era.
	- ID: 2026101301161847
	- Type: Enhancement
	- Status: Done
	- Needs external testing: Done on emulated Penryn and Prescott, and the macOS scan on b26. Not run on a real Core 2 Duo or Phenom II, and not on an emulated AMD chip. Windows and FreeBSD get the static scan only.
	- Priority [Feature|Enhancement] | Severity [Bug]: Avg
	- Opened: 20261013-011618
	- Opened by: JC
	- Target OS: Linux, Windows and FreeBSD on x86_64, where this project builds them. Macs that old can't run a current macOS anyway.
	- Requirements [Feature]:
		- Release builds run on the last Core 2 Duo (Penryn, 2008), and on AMD Phenom II and Athlon II, if that's reasonably doable.
			- Penryn has SSSE3 and SSE4.1 but no POPCNT. The AMD ones have POPCNT but no SSSE3. So the shared floor is plain x86-64, also called x86-64-v1.
		- If that isn't doable, the floor is the first Core i3/i5/i7 (Nehalem, 2008), which is x86-64-v2. AMD's first chip at that level is Bulldozer, 2011.
		- Faster code for newer CPUs is still fine, but it's picked at run time by a CPU feature test, never by the build target.
		- Tests run the release build on an emulated Penryn and Phenom II, through Intel SDE or QEMU's `-cpu Penryn` and `-cpu phenom`.
	- Progress log:
		- 20261013: Release builds name a Zig target since 2026093018112406, so they get the baseline CPU, and ddw1c fails on AVX (2026101218531694). What's left:
			- ddw1c only looks for AVX. Widen it to anything past the floor.
			- Zig picks its SHA-256 code at compile time, so a baseline build never uses SHA-NI, even where it's there. A run-time pick needs both versions built in.
			- Wasmtime is a prebuilt archive. Find out what CPU it needs, and what it does on one without SSE4.1.
		- 20261013: The scan now fails on anything past x86-64-v1, not just AVX, at all 4 places it ran: the dogfood build, and the Linux, FreeBSD and Windows releases. Penryn has no POPCNT and Phenom II no SSSE3, so both are refused, and so are LZCNT, BMI, AES-NI, SHA and the rest.
			- TZCNT is let through. LLVM writes BSF that way on a baseline build, where the answer is the same.
			- zstd's BMI2 copies of a few functions are skipped like Wasmtime's Rust, since zstd checks the CPU before it calls them.
			- The macOS x86_64 slice is held to Core 2 instead, Zig's baseline there, which has SSSE3. Held to x86-64-v1 it would fail on b26. Not run on a Mac yet.
		- 20261013: Cranelift has run wasm SIMD on plain SSE2 since Wasmtime 11, so 47.0.3 and FreeBSD's 49.0.1 need nothing past the floor, and the engine config stays as it is.
		- 20261013: New checks run the Linux release under Intel's SDE as a Penryn and as a Prescott, the nearest it has to Phenom II, which also lacks SSSE3 and SSE4.1. Each takes about 6 s. They skip when there's no `sde64`, or `ZUID_SDE` to name one.
		- 20261013: they also look in `~/.local/opt/intel-sde`, where SDE was unpacked here, so a plain run on b23 doesn't skip them. Nothing was put on PATH.
			- SDE stops on an instruction the chip lacks, in compiled wasm code too. It doesn't catch LZCNT, which the scan does.
			- Each run starts with a module cache made on a newer CPU. Wasmtime turns it down and compiles again.
		- 20261013: dde03 failed on b26 in 29 functions, such as zstd's `HIST_count_simple` on `pmaxud`. That is zstd inside Wasmtime's macOS archive, built for Penryn, as Rust's `x86_64-apple-darwin` target is. It doesn't ask the CPU first, so the Linux exceptions don't cover it. Every Mac that runs macOS 13 has far more than Penryn, so the scan is wrong rather than the release. Back to Queued.
		- 20261013: the scan now finds SDE in `~/.local/opt/intel-sde` too, and the full b23 run had ddwkf and ddwkg pass with nothing set.
		- 20261013: Of the 29, 26 are zstd functions that use SSE4.1 and nothing newer, such as `pmaxud`, `pminud`, `pmovzxbq`, `pinsrb` and `blendvps`. The other 3 are labels inside zstd's BMI2 assembly loops, `.L_4X1_loop_body` and the like, which only run when the CPU has BMI2. A Mac's debug file keeps those labels as symbols, so the scan took them for functions.
		- 20261013: The macOS x86_64 slice is held to Penryn now, Core 2 plus SSE4.1. The labels count as part of the function they're in, as they do on Linux. Linux, FreeBSD and Windows stay at x86-64-v1.
	- Decisions:
		- 20261013: No run-time SHA-NI pick in Zig's SHA-256. It hashes a few names per run, so there's nothing to gain. The requirement allows faster code but doesn't ask for it, and Wasmtime's own SHA-256 already picks at run time.
		- 20261013: Prescott stands in for Phenom II under SDE, which has no AMD chips. It lacks SSSE3 and SSE4.1 as Phenom II does, and POPCNT, which Phenom II has.
	- Branch: oldcpu2, macfix
	- Commit: 4bbd035, 7218b59, 119c277
	- Test case: ddwkf and ddwkg, the release under emulated Penryn and Prescott. ddw1c, dde03, ddgez and ddgpb now scan for anything past x86-64-v1.
		- On a Mac, dde03 scans the x86_64 slice for anything past Penryn. It failed on b26 at 2fa8524 in 29 functions against Core 2, and passes now. Watched fail on b26: a build for that Mac's own CPU, 279 functions, and the release without the label change, on the 3 BMI2 labels. Watched fail: `x86_64_v2` builds for Linux, FreeBSD and Windows failed the scan on SSE4.1 and SSSE3 code, which the AVX-only scan passed, and failed ddwkf on `popcnt`. A build for this CPU failed the scan in 282 functions. A Penryn build passed ddwkf and failed ddwkg on `pshufb`.
	- Swept: all 4 callers of the old AVX scan in `cicd.bash`, the Windows one through its PDB. The arm64 scan has its own list. No other script calls it.
		- The Penryn floor is passed only by dde03, for macOS. The label change is in `fDisassemble`, which the arm64 scan uses too; ELF files have no `.L` labels. The x86-64-v1 filter gives the same output as before on SSE4.1, SSSE3, POPCNT, SSE4.2 and AVX lines.
	- Verified: `cicd.bash --package --no-dogfood --no-backup -q` passed on b23 with SDE, 20261013, at 4bbd035 and again at 7218b59, 342 ok and 5 skipped for macOS or Windows.
	- Acceptance signoff: Self-closed 20261013. Emulated Penryn and Prescott passed, and the scans fail on anything past the floor. Penryn for the Mac scan changes nothing a user sees, since every Mac on macOS 13 is far past it. A real Core 2 Duo or Phenom II can reopen it.
	- Closed: 20261013-131118
		- `cicd.bash --quick --only zig --package` passed on b26 at 119c277, 20261013, dde03 included. On b23, `cicd.bash --quick` passed, 306 ok, and `--quick --only zig --package` passed, 307 ok, with dde03, ddgez, ddgpb, ddfaa, ddwkf and ddwkg.

- `--salt` shows the secret in the process list.
	- ID: 2026100519343984
	- Type: Enhancement
	- Status: Done
	- Priority [Feature|Enhancement] | Severity [Bug]: Avg
	- Opened: 20261005-193439
	- Opened by: JC
	- Target OS: Any
	- Requirements  [Feature]:
		- A way to give the salt that other users on the machine cannot read.
	- Progress log:
		- 20261005: `--salt-file <path>` reads it from a file, or from stdin for `-`. One trailing newline is dropped, `\r\n` included. An empty file, a missing one, one over 256 bytes, and both flags at once are refused.
	- Decisions:
		- 20261005: a file, not an environment variable. A variable changes the output with nothing on the command line to show it, which is the trouble a config file had.
		- 20261005: an empty file is refused, since it would give unsalted output to someone who thinks it is salted. `--salt ''` still means no salt.
	- Branch: fastcli
	- Test case: ddj7p to ddj7w in `cli-test.bash`. ddj7r, ddj7s and ddj7w failed with the `\r` strip, the empty check and the both-flags check taken out.

	- Closed: 20261013-033301

- One table for error messages across the command, the C module and the Go module.
	- ID: 2026100519343989
	- Type: Enhancement
	- Status: Done
	- Priority [Feature|Enhancement] | Severity [Bug]: Avg
	- Opened: 20261005-193439
	- Opened by: JC
	- Related IDs: 2026100519343980
	- Target OS: Any
	- Requirements  [Feature]:
		- The command's `dieGenerating`, the C module's `textFor` and the Go module's `fmt.Errorf` calls each word the same failures, and they drift. Message style has been fixed in 2 rounds already.
		- One source for each kind's text, or a test that holds the three to it.
	- Progress log:
		- 20261005: Asked for after a design review.
		- 20261005: `testdata/errors.tsv` has a row per failure, with its C code, its Go `Err`, the core errors behind it and a phrase each surface's message has to contain. The Go tests, the Zig tests and `cli-test.bash` all read it.
		- 20261005: the command's generation messages moved to `zig/cmd/src/messages.zig`, so the Zig tests can check each one, including the ones no flag reaches.
		- 20261005: not every surface words every failure, and the table says which. Go names the cause of an env failure instead, the command has no hash width flag, and the conversion library's own text is passed through as before.
		- 20261005: wording that moved, for signoff. No Go text changed.
			- C, width out of range: "a symbol count is out of range" now ends ": want 1 to 64", as Go and the command already say.
			- C, refused base: "renders raw bytes rather than text" is now "raw bytes or control characters", as the command says. 98keyboard was told it renders raw bytes.
			- Command, clock past the horizon: it had no message and printed "Generation failed: WidthOverflow." Now "The clock is past the padding horizon, so the timestamp no longer fits its fixed width.", as C says.
	- Decisions:
		- 20261005: a test against a shared phrase, not one source of text. Each surface keeps its own style, so the command's style guide and the library style both still hold.
	- Note: `ZUID_ERR_INTERNAL` (7) is in `zuid.h`, but nothing returns it now.
	- Branch: errtable
	- Commit: 698dc9a
	- Swept: every core error and every code in `zuid.h` has a row, checked both ways. So does every `Err` in `zuid.go`, under the C name its comment gives. The command's own flag checks for precision, `--rand-chars` and the salt are held to the table in `cli-test.bash`.
	- Test case: ddj8d in `zuid_test.go`, ddj8e, ddj8f and ddj8g in `tests.zig`, and ddj8k to ddj8w in `cli-test.bash`. Each failed with a fault planted: a Go text, an `Err` comment or a new `Err` with no row; a `codeFor` code, a dropped row or a wrong code against `zuid.h`; the old C base text; the command's horizon message taken out; a flag message reworded.
	- Verified: the Go and Zig tests, `cli-test.bash`, the test ID check, the format check and shellcheck all pass.
	- Acceptance signoff: Self-closed: the 3 changed messages copy wording another surface already used, so nothing new was worded.
	- Closed: 20261013-033301

- Update vmFreeBSD's kernel and every package, then retry the panic.

	- Priority [Feature|Enhancement] | Severity [Bug]: Avg

	- Opened by: JC
	- Parent ID: 2026100413383471
	- Target OS: FreeBSD 15.1, x86_64
	- Requirements  [Feature]:
		- Update the base system and kernel with FreeBSD's own updater, and every package with `pkg upgrade`. Reboot.
		- Rerun `cli-test.bash` against the release on the VM, logged to disk as before, to see whether the panic still shows up.
	- Progress log:
		- 20261004: The VM's base system is pkgbase, not distribution sets, so the base and kernel update through `pkg` and not `freebsd-update`. After a forced catalog refresh, `pkg upgrade` found nothing to do in the base, ports or kmods repos. Base, kernel and userland were already at 15.1-RELEASE-p4, the same before and after, with pkg 2.7.5. They were installed when the VM was built, so the panic also happened on p4. With nothing installed, there was no reboot.
		- 20261004: Retest against the release built from 7dbdc21, as the unprivileged test user, with every zuid call logged to disk: `cli-test.bash` passed 69 of 69 in 16 runs one after another and in 24 runs four at a time, with the 30 Zig tests run after each of those 24 and passing every time. `capi_smoke.c` built with the base `cc` against the shared and the static library and passed both ways. No panic and no new crash dump.
		- 20261004: The FreeBSD tarball has `share/LICENSE-wasmtime.txt`.
	- Test case: none. A check of the test machine, not of zuid; the runs above are the result.
	- Acceptance signoff: Self-closed: the update check and the retest ran.
	- Closed: 20261004-183600

- `install.bash` can't remove a folder where `rm` is BusyBox's.
	- ID: 2026102618305629
	- Type: Bug
	- Status: Done
	- Priority [Feature|Enhancement] | Severity [Bug]: Low
	- Opened: 20261026-183056
	- Opened by: JC
	- Related IDs: 2026102407393000
	- Target OS: Linux, BusyBox userland such as Alpine
	- Steps to reproduce [Bug]:
		- In the `bash:3.2` image, with HOME holding `~/.cache/zuid`, run `install.bash --uninstall --target user --yes`.
	- Incorrect behavior [Bug]:
		- `rm: unrecognized option: x`, then "couldn't remove", exit 1.
	- Expected behavior [Bug]:
		- The folder is removed, or refused by name with a reason.
	- Reproduced [Bug]: 20261026 on b23, for the cache dir.
	- Possible cause [Bug]:
		- `fSafeRm_Under` gives BSD's `-x` to any `rm` without `--version`. BusyBox has neither that nor `--one-file-system`. An install dir's `bin` goes through the same call, unchecked.
	- Needs local test suite run?: none.
	- Needs external testing: none.
	- Progress log:
		- 20261027: `install.bash` gives `rm` `--one-file-system` for GNU, `-x` on macOS and FreeBSD, and no flag otherwise. The guard's checks (physical parent, no link, strictly under it) already cover what the flag guarded.
	- Decisions:
		- 20261027: The flag follows the OS where `rm --version` fails, so BusyBox on Linux is the only case that goes without. Version stays 1.4.0, as it has not shipped.
	- Swept: every `rm -r` in `install.bash` goes through `fSafeRm_Under`, the install dir's entries and the cache included. `install.ps1` has no `rm`. The other scripts that use `-x` or `--one-file-system` run under the pipeline's own guard, not from a download.
	- Branch: round1027
	- Test case: defpx (BusyBox `rm` in the `bash:3.2` image removes the install dir and the cache). Failed against the old `install.bash`, passes now.
	- Verified: 20261027, `installer-test.bash` 108 of 108, shellcheck clean apart from existing SC1091 info notes.

- `install.ps1` wraps its link refusal in the generic write error.
	- ID: 2026102516500000
	- Type: Bug
	- Status: Done
	- Needs local test suite run?: none.
	- Priority [Feature|Enhancement] | Severity [Bug]: Low
	- Opened: 20261025-165000
	- Opened by: JC
	- Related IDs: 2026102514072150
	- Steps to reproduce [Bug]: Make the install dir a junction, then run `install.ps1 -Target system`.
	- Expected [Bug]: One line saying the install dir is a link and was left alone.
	- Actual [Bug]: "couldn't write C:\Program Files\zuid: zuid-install: refused to empty C:\Program Files\zuid: it's a link Close any running zuid, check the permissions, or try -Target user." The prefix shows twice, and the advice is for a different problem.
	- Reproduced [Bug]: 20261025, with `installer-test.bash` on Linux, and on b29w under 5.1 and 7, user and system. `install.bash` had the same fault: its refusal came out ahead of "couldn't write ... Check its permissions".
	- Actual cause [Bug]: `Clear-InstallDirectory` throws the refusal inside the `try` around the install's writes, and that `catch` wraps any error in the write message. `install.bash` printed its refusal and returned 1, and its caller then died with the write message.
	- Actual fix [Bug]: `install.ps1` rethrows its own messages from that `catch` as they are. `install.bash` dies with the refusal itself. Both say "refused to empty <dir>: it's a link, so it was left alone." Both installers are 1.3.2.
	- Branch: round1026
	- Test case: deea3 and deea4 in `installer-test.bash`, for bash and ps1, plus the junction checks in deea5 on b29w. Against the old scripts deea3 failed on the write advice and deea4 on the doubled prefix, and the b29w junction checks failed too. All pass after.
	- Verified: 20261025, `installer-test.bash` 99 of 99, and deea5 on b29w, 33 of 33.
	- Swept: both refusals in `Clear-InstallDirectory` and `fEmptyInstallDir`, and both of `fEmptyInstallDir`'s callers, install and uninstall. `install.ps1`'s uninstall calls `Clear-InstallDirectory` with no wrap. `Remove-Under`'s refusals now come through unwrapped too.
	- Acceptance signoff: Self-closed 20261025. Checked on Linux and on b29w.
	- Closed: 20261025-190430

- Comments and notes say the image has no `ionice`, but it does.
	- ID: 2026102509054739
	- Type: Bug
	- Status: Done
	- Priority [Feature|Enhancement] | Severity [Bug]: Low
	- Opened: 20261025-090547
	- Opened by: JC
	- Related IDs: 2026102416233102, 2026102508540624
	- Target OS: Linux
	- Steps to reproduce [Bug]:
		- Run `command -v ionice` in `zuid-cicd:8029eec721c8`.
	- Incorrect behavior [Bug]: It's `/usr/bin/ionice`, from util-linux. `fInImage` in `cicd.bash` says "The image has no ionice" and runs only `nice -n 19` inside, and 2026102416233102's log says the same.
	- Expected behavior [Bug]: Either the containers also get `ionice -c 3` inside, or the comment says why not.
	- Reproduced [Bug]: Yes, 20261025, on b23. Preflight inside the image printed "IO idle".
	- Possible cause [Bug]: The comment was written from an assumption, not a check.
	- Actual cause [Bug]: As above. The image, and the CodeQL image, both have `/usr/bin/ionice`.
	- Actual fix [Bug]: The three `docker run` lines for the images in `cicd.bash` (the container stage, the CodeQL run, and `fInImage`) now start `ionice -c 3 nice -n 19`. The wrong comment is corrected.
	- Decisions:
		- 20261025: 2026102416233102's log line "The image has no ionice" is closed history and stays. This item is the correction.
	- Swept: every `ionice` and `nice -n` in `cicd/`. The `bash:3.2` runs in `installer-test.bash` keep plain `nice`, since that is a different image. Both zuid images answered `command -v ionice`.

	- Test case: The `run` line asserts in `pipeline-test.bash` (ddrj1, de5ea, dedm0 family, dedp8, and the cache cap and ps cases) now want `ionice -c 3 nice -n 19`. Updated for the change this item asked for, not newly written.

- `cli-test.bash` fails ddj75 when `--bin` is a relative path.
	- ID: 2026102321183047
	- Type: Bug
	- Status: Done
	- Priority [Feature|Enhancement] | Severity [Bug]: Low
	- Opened: 20261023-211830
	- Opened by: JC
	- Related IDs: 2026102318361186
	- Steps to reproduce [Bug]:
		- From `repo/`, run `bash cicd/utility/cli-test.bash --bin zig/zig-out/bin/zuid`.
	- Incorrect behavior [Bug]: ddj75 fails with "nothing under .../home/.cache/zuid", 89 of 90.
	- Expected behavior [Bug]: 90 of 90, as with the same build's absolute path.
	- Reproduced [Bug]: Yes, 20261023 on b23, 3 runs of 3. The absolute path passed.
	- Possible cause [Bug]: The test runs `${bin}` after `cd "${work}"`, so a relative path names nothing there, and its `|| true` hides that. The round settings in memory.md give the relative form.
	- Actual cause [Bug]:
		- As guessed. A relative `--bin` named nothing after the `cd`, and `|| true` hid the failed run.
	- Actual fix [Bug]: `cli-test.bash` makes `--bin` absolute before any case runs. ddj75 also fails on the run's exit code now, and says it.
	- Verified: `--bin zig/zig-out/bin/zuid` from `repo/` failed 89 of 90 before the fix and passed 90 of 90 after. So did `./zig-out/bin/zuid` from `zig/`, and the absolute path still passed. debnh failed against the old `cli-test.bash` and passes with the fix.
	- Swept: every `cd` in `cli-test.bash`; ddj75 is the only case that runs the command from another dir. The `macos` workflow passes a relative `--bin` too, but ddj75 skips on a Mac.
	- Branch: round1023
	- Commit: faef0ac
	- Test case: debnh, in `pipeline-test.bash`, runs `cli-test.bash` with a relative `--bin` to a stand-in that keeps a module the way the command does, and wants ddj75 to pass. It skips on a Mac.
	- Acceptance signoff: Self-closed: mechanical fix, and the test failed before it and passes after.
	- Closed: 20261023-214307

- Container runs stamp artifacts in UTC, so they sort apart from host runs.
	- ID: 2026101912130116
	- Type: Bug
	- Status: Done
	- Needs local test suite run?: Done. `cicd.bash --container --only go --quick --leakcheck` passed, and the soak profile made inside has the same stamp as the host's run log.
	- Priority [Feature|Enhancement] | Severity [Bug]: Low
	- Opened: 20261019-121301
	- Opened by: JC
	- Related IDs: 2026101816474402
	- Incorrect behavior [Bug]: Inside `--container` the run stamp is UTC, so leakcheck reports and profiles from a container run are named hours off from a host run's and sort out of order.
	- Expected behavior [Bug]: One clock for every run's file names.
	- Reproduced [Bug]: Seen in the `--leakcheck` work, 20261019. de5gg failed on the tree before the fix.
	- Actual cause [Bug]: The image has no time zone data and gets no `TZ`, so `date` inside reads UTC. The host has no `TZ` either, since its zone comes from `/etc/localtime`, so handing a zone name in wouldn't have helped.
	- Actual fix [Bug]: The run outside hands its own stamp to the run inside, which names every file with it. A malformed one is ignored, and so is one in the environment of a host run.
	- Swept: every use of the run stamp in `cicd.bash`: the run log, the sync stash, the Zig crash copy, the leak checks, CodeQL, the profiles and the demo. They all read the one stamp. `package.bash` dates from `SOURCE_DATE_EPOCH`, which was already handed in, and `test-ids.py` is UTC on purpose.
	- Branch: cicdtidy
	- Commit: 04a10ae
	- Test case: de5gg. It failed before the fix and passes after, and fails again with either guard taken out. ddrj1's expected `docker run` line has the stamp in it now.
	- Acceptance signoff: Self-closed: its test failed before the fix and passes after.
	- Closed: 20261019-124500

- `pipeline-test.bash` leaves 2 temp folders behind on every run.
	- ID: 2026101314215041
	- Type: Bug
	- Status: Done
	- Priority|Severity [Bug]: Low
	- Opened: 20261013-142150
	- Opened by: JC
	- Target OS: Any
	- Steps to reproduce [Bug]:
		- Run `pipeline-test.bash`, then look in the temp folder for new `tmp.*` folders holding only a `fuzz.log`.
	- Incorrect behavior [Bug]: 2 are left per run, from the fuzz find and broken fuzz cases. The find's log names a test that "terminated with signal ABRT", so a leftover reads like a real crash. 46 had built up by 20261013.
	- Expected behavior [Bug]: A test run leaves nothing behind.
	- Reproduced [Bug]: Yes, 20261013, on b23. The logs match the stand-in zig's output, not a real fuzz run.
	- Possible cause [Bug]: `fFuzzIn` runs `fStage_Zig_Fuzz` inside `$( )`. The stage adds its log folder to `_scratchDirs` in that subshell, and the parent's cleanup never sees it. A failed `cicd.bash` check may also leave its stamp folder, seen while working 2026101313551112.
	- Actual cause [Bug]:
		- The fuzz cases run the stage in a subshell with no cleanup of their own. The find and broken cases exit there, so their log folder stays. A clean run removes its own.
		- `cicd.bash` only cleaned scratch folders under `/tmp`. With TMPDIR set elsewhere, as here and on macOS, every run kept the Zig link folder, and a failed check kept its stamp, package or smoke folder too. 30 or so of those are in the temp dir from 20261013.
	- Actual fix [Bug]:
		- The fuzz cases run the script's own cleanup when their subshell exits, with TMPDIR pointed inside the harness's work folder.
		- Cleanup takes any `tmp.*` folder directly in TMPDIR or `/tmp`, and nothing else. No links, and no folder that `mktemp -d` did not name.
		- 46 leftover fuzz folders were removed from the temp dir. The other `cicd.bash` leftovers were left alone.
	- Verified:
		- Before: `pipeline-test.bash` left 2 `fuzz.log` folders, and `cicd.bash --quick --no-backup` left 3, the third a `zig` link folder. A forced `fThrowError` in `cicd.bash` left its folder.
		- After: both leave nothing, and so do a forced `fThrowError` and a failed command. `pipeline-test.bash` passes 60 of 60 with TMPDIR set and unset. The quick run passes.
		- Swept: `fFuzzIn` is the only `$( )` or subshell call in `pipeline-test.bash` that runs a stage with a `mktemp -d`. `cli-test.bash` and `installer-test.bash` lift no stages. All 7 `mktemp -d` in `cicd.bash` add to `_scratchDirs` right away, and `fThrowError` and `fTestFail` both exit through the cleanup trap. No `$( )` helper in `cicd.bash` makes a temp folder.
	- Branch: tmpleak
	- Commit: cfc2014, c2046c0
	- Test case: ddx66 (fuzz cases leave nothing in TMPDIR) and ddx67 (cleanup takes `tmp.*` in TMPDIR and nothing else). Both failed before the fix and pass after.
	- Acceptance signoff: Self-closed 20261013. Cleanup still takes only the folders the run made itself and listed, and the tests failed before the fix.
	- Closed: 20261013-143229

- `ZUID_ERR_INTERNAL` is never returned, and a runtime trap comes back as `ZUID_ERR_CONVERT`.
	- ID: 2026100520074635
	- Type: Bug
	- Status: Done
	- Priority [Feature|Enhancement] | Severity [Bug]: Low
	- Opened: 20261005-200746
	- Opened by: JC
	- Related IDs: 2026100519343989, 2026100519343982
	- Target OS: Any
	- Incorrect behavior [Bug]: `zuid.h` says code 7 means the embedded runtime or module failed. A Wasmtime error or trap during a call maps to `ConvertFailed`, which is code 4, and the NULL context that used to return 7 now has code 14. So nothing returns 7.
	- Expected behavior [Bug]: A runtime failure says so, or code 7 is retired in place like 3 and 11.
	- Reproduced [Bug]: Yes. A Wasmtime error from a call with the wrong number of arguments, and the module's Go runtime exiting on an alloc it had no memory for, both came back as `ConvertFailed`, code 4. The exit's text was 512 bytes of backtrace, cut off before the cause.
	- Actual cause [Bug]: `call()` in `host.zig` turned every Wasmtime error and trap into `ConvertFailed`, and `capi.zig` gave that code 4. The module's own internal error and a pointer past its memory did the same. Wasmtime puts the backtrace ahead of the cause in its message.
	- Progress log:
		- 20261005: Found while building the error table.
		- 20261005: No request reaches a runtime failure. The module checks every pointer and length it is handed, and it keeps working after its runtime exits, so the test calls exports the wrong way on purpose.
	- Decisions:
		- 20261005: code 7 is made true rather than retired. Its comment in `zuid.h` already said what it is for, and still does.
		- 20261005: the module's own error 7, `Internal`, is code 7 too. The reactor's README says it means the base registry failed to build, which is the module breaking rather than refusing a request.
		- 20261005: a pointer the module hands back past its own memory is code 7 too, and so is a failure reading its error text. These used to be code 4 with no text at all.
		- 20261005: `ConvertFailed` is gone, since nothing produced it after that. Code 4 is now only the library refusing the input.
	- Actual fix [Bug]: A new core error, `RuntimeFailed`, which `capi.zig` maps to 7. The runtime's text keeps Wasmtime's cause and drops the backtrace. With no text, C says "the embedded wasm runtime or its module failed" and the command says "The embedded wasm runtime or its module failed." `errors.tsv` has the row, with `-` for Go, which has no runtime.
		- For signoff: a C caller that got 4 for a runtime failure now gets 7, and the text for one no longer has the backtrace.
	- Branch: trap7
	- Commit: dd8fdcc
	- Swept: every `ConvertFailed` in `host.zig`, `core.zig`, `capi.zig`, `messages.zig` and `errors.tsv`. `core.zig`'s own `BadInput` for a random draw past its buffer stays code 4, since it is a core limit and not the runtime. `zuid.h`'s comments for 4 and 7 still hold. `design.md`, `capi_smoke.c` and the Go side name neither code. A runtime that fails to start still makes `zuid_new` return NULL, as the header says.
	- Test case: ddj93 in `host.zig`, pulled into the test binary from `tests.zig`. It failed with `call()` put back to code 4, and again with the backtrace kept. ddj8e, ddj8f and ddj8g hold the new row.
	- Verified: the format check, the build, the Zig tests, the Go tests, `cli-test.bash` and the test ID check all pass.
	- Acceptance signoff: Self-closed: code 7 now means what `zuid.h` always said, the test failed before the fix, and no request reaches it.
	- Closed: 20261013-033301

- `cicd.bash` refuses to run in a git worktree.
	- ID: 2026100318410007
	- Type: Bug
	- Status: Done
	- Priority|Severity [Bug]: Low
	- Opened: 20261003-184100
	- Opened by: JC
	- Target OS: Any
	- Steps to reproduce [Bug]:
		- `git worktree add <dir> main`, then run `cicd/cicd.bash` there.
	- Incorrect behavior [Bug]: It stops at once with "Not a git repo".
	- Expected behavior [Bug]: It runs, since a worktree is a full checkout.
	- Reproduced [Bug]: Yes, 20261003.
	- Possible cause [Bug]: The sync and preflight checks look for a `.git` directory, and in a worktree `.git` is a file.
	- Actual cause [Bug]: As above. Both checks tested for a `.git` directory.
	- Progress log:
		- 20261004: The backup stage is skipped in a linked worktree, with a line saying so, and `--backup` asked for by name is refused there. The helper archives the folder above the checkout, which for a worktree is not the project, and it wants a real `.git` directory. Making the backup work from a worktree would mean archiving the main checkout's project instead, which is a bigger change than this item.
		- 20261004: Verified: `cicd.bash --quick --no-dogfood -q` from a scratch worktree passed, with the backup skipped. `--backup` there was refused before any stage. `pipeline-test.bash` 39 of 39, `test-ids.py check` and shellcheck pass.
	- Decisions:
		- Skip the backup in a worktree rather than make it work there. Confirmed 20261004.
	- Actual fix [Bug]: Sync and preflight ask git whether the folder is the top of a checkout. Argument handling spots a linked worktree by its git dir differing from the common one.
	- Swept: every `.git` path test in `cicd/` and the rest of the repo. `package.bash` and the test harnesses had none. The vendored `n8git_backup-and-publish` tests `.git/HEAD` and stays as is, since `cicd.bash` no longer calls it from a worktree.
	- Branch: worktree
	- Commit: 9ff63b7
	- Test case: ddgkv, ddgkw, ddgky and ddgkz in `pipeline-test.bash`, which all failed before the fix. ddgkx checks a folder that is not a checkout's top is still refused.
	- Acceptance signoff: Self-closed: it runs in a worktree, and skipping the backup there was confirmed.
	- Closed: 20261004-184405

- `test-ids.py check` missed test IDs kept in an array.

	- Status: Done

	- Opened by: JC
	- Related IDs: 2026100317515523
	- Steps to reproduce [Bug]:

	- Incorrect behavior [Bug]: It passes without reading those IDs, so a duplicate or a malformed one would go through.
	- Expected behavior [Bug]: Every ID in the pipeline is checked, or the check fails.
	- Reproduced [Bug]: 20261004, on `ddfac` and `ddfad` in `cicd.bash`.
	- Actual cause [Bug]:
		- The check only reads IDs at the start of an `fId` or `fWant*` line, and the array put them somewhere else.
	- Actual fix [Bug]:
		- The arm64 link checks use `fId`'s keyed form, like the cross targets.
		- The check also fails on a word that looks like an ID but that no test line names, and on a `// test-id:` mark that is not above a test. A comment is let through, so a retired test can stay in one.
	- Branch: idgaps
	- Test case: `ddg6y` to `ddg72` in `pipeline-test.bash`, against a scratch tree. `ddg6z`, `ddg70` and `ddg72` failed on the old `test-ids.py`. `ddg71` failed with the comment exemption taken out.
	- Closed: 20261004-090044

- The Linux, macOS and FreeBSD tarballs have `libwasmtime.a` without Wasmtime's license.
	- ID: 2026100417324819
	- Type: Bug
	- Status: Done
	- Needs local test suite run?: No. A full `cicd.bash --package --no-dogfood --no-backup -q` passed on Linux with the fix.
	- Needs external testing: Done on b26, 20261004.
	- Priority|Severity [Bug]: Low
	- Opened: 20261004-173248
	- Opened by: JC
	- Related IDs: 2026100416052036
	- Steps to reproduce [Bug]:
		- List any `.tgz` from `--package`.
	- Incorrect behavior [Bug]:
		- `lib/libwasmtime.a` is there, and nothing in `share/` is Wasmtime's Apache-2.0 license, which has to go along with a copy of it.
	- Expected behavior [Bug]:
		- `share/LICENSE-wasmtime.txt`, as the Windows zips have. FreeBSD's vendored package keeps no license file yet, so that fetch needs one too.
	- Reproduced [Bug]: 20261004, on the Linux tarballs, `.deb` and `.rpm` from `main`. The command links Wasmtime in statically, so the packages lacked it too, and they also lacked the module's Apache license and NOTICE.
	- Actual cause [Bug]:
		- `package.bash` copied Wasmtime's license only for the Windows zips. The FreeBSD fetch kept zstd's license and not Wasmtime's.
	- Actual fix [Bug]:
		- Every tarball and zip gets `share/LICENSE-wasmtime.txt`, from `LICENSE` in the vendored Wasmtime tree. Linux, macOS and Windows take it from Wasmtime's own pinned archive. FreeBSD's `libwasmtime` package keeps it as `share/licenses/libwasmtime-*/APACHE20`, which is Wasmtime's `LICENSE` word for word, and the fetch now copies it in as `LICENSE`. A FreeBSD tree vendored before this has no `LICENSE`, so it is fetched again.
		- The `.deb` and `.rpm` also get `LICENSE-wasmtime.txt`, `LICENSE-module.txt` and `NOTICE.txt`, beside the GPL text they had.
		- The module license and NOTICE stay in the packages, 20261004.
		- Note: the bare binaries still go out with no license file beside them, as before.
	- Verified: the full `--package` run above. The FreeBSD fetch ran again and its `LICENSE` matches Wasmtime's own. Both macOS archives at the pinned checksums have the same `LICENSE`. Every new check failed against the release built from `main`, and the tarball check failed with the module's license in place of Wasmtime's.
	- Swept: every tarball, both zips, both `.deb` and both `.rpm`. The Windows check now reads for the text only Wasmtime's license has, as the new checks do. `install.bash` and `install.ps1` install the whole tree, so nothing changes there. The macOS tarball change is only read, not run.
	- Branch: wtlicense
	- Commit: 2dd0931
	- Test case: ddgq4 to ddgqb, one per release: the Linux x86_64 and arm64, FreeBSD and macOS tarballs, and each `.deb` and `.rpm`. Each Linux one failed on `main`'s release and passes now. ddgq7 runs only on a Mac and has not run.
	- Verified: 20261004 on b26, at 12d2b2d: ddgq7 passed in the full `--package` run, and the macOS tarball lists `share/LICENSE-wasmtime.txt`, which is the Apache 2.0 text. One test fixture line was changed for that run, for an unrelated failure logged as 2026100417561172.
	- Acceptance signoff: Self-closed: every release has the license, and its tests pass.
	- Closed: 20261004-175611

- The C soak never runs `%c`.
	- ID: 2026102520060700
	- Type: Enhancement
	- Status: Done
	- Priority [Feature|Enhancement] | Severity [Bug]: Low
	- Opened: 20261025-200607
	- Opened by: JC
	- Related IDs: 2026102409130649
	- Requirements  [Feature]:
		- `capi_soak.c` adds `%c` to its formats, so the counter the C context now keeps runs under heaptrack.
		- Its loop must stay under the 2^20 limit per tick, or pin the clock forward between rounds.
	- Progress log:
		- 20261027: The call soak has 3 `%c` formats, alone, with `%d` and `%r`, and with `%h` and `%g`. Each round moves the pinned clock 1000 s forward, which starts the counter over, so a long soak never reaches 2^20.
	- Decisions:
		- 20261027: No new test ID. The soak's test, de5ch, now covers `%c`; a failed `%c` call fails the round.
	- Test case: de5ch and de5cm, unchanged IDs.
	- Verified:
		- 20261027: The soak built with the pipeline's flags against the current library and passed plain, at 20 and the default 100 rounds, with the heap level between N and 2N.
		- Not verified: under heaptrack, and the soak failing on a broken `%c`.
	- Verified: 20261027, de5ch and de5cm PASS in the Leak check step of a plain `cicd/cicd.bash --backlog-run-full` on round1027 at 0ac99bd.
	- Swept: capi_smoke.c and capi_interpose.c, the other C files that call `zuid_generate`, make a few calls each and no loop of them, so neither nears the limit.

- The FreeBSD `.pkg`, made on vmFreeBSD.
	- ID: 2026102617244796
	- Type: Enhancement
	- Status: Done
	- Priority [Feature|Enhancement] | Severity [Bug]: Low
	- Opened: 20261026-172447
	- Opened by: JC
	- Parent ID: 2026101410500001
	- Target OS: FreeBSD
	- Requirements  [Feature]:
		- `--package` lays out what the package holds, and the FreeBSD box step runs `pkg create` on it there, under the host lock, and brings the `.pkg` home beside the tarball.
		- The box checks the package before it comes home: its ABI, its version, and that it installs and runs.
		- A taken or down VM is a skip, as for the other box steps.
	- Progress log:
		- 20261026: `package.bash` lays out the package in `dist/pkg/<release>/`: the command and its licenses under `root/usr/local`, as in the `.deb`, with a manifest, a plist and the commit's time. `--publish` reads only the top of `dist/`, so none of it is a release file.
		- 20261026: The FreeBSD box step then sends that to the same VM, where `box-freebsd-pkg.bash` runs `pkg create`, checks the name, version, ABI and file list pkg reads back, installs it with `pkg add` under a scratch root and runs the installed command. The `.pkg` comes home to `dist/` and its `checksums.txt`.
		- 20261026: `pkg add` as `bsdtest` fails, since it sets each file's owner to root, so the job runs as `root@vmFreeBSD`. The scratch root gets copies of the base libraries the package needs, or pkg calls them missing.
	- Decisions:
		- 20261026: The ABI is `FreeBSD:*:amd64`. pkg 2.7.5 refuses `FreeBSD:14:amd64` on 15 as the wrong architecture, and matches the ABI as a pattern. Built for 14.0, it runs on 14 and later. Left alone, `pkg create` stamps the build box's own, 15.
		- 20261026: The version follows the ports rule, so 1.0.0-beta.1 is `1.0.0.b.1`, which pkg sorts before 1.0.0. The file keeps the release's name, `zuid-<version>-freebsd-x86_64.pkg`, so the release table puts it in FreeBSD's row.
		- 20261026: Its own job, so the VM boots twice in the step, about 25 s more. A box that skipped the release test skips this without asking again.
		- 20261026: Into `dist/`, not `dist-incoming/`. It is this run's, and `dist/` is emptied each run, so an old one can't be published by mistake.
		- 20261026: Not in `default_releaseAssets` yet, or `--publish` would refuse whenever the VM is down. 2026102617244801 adds it.
		- 20261026: A missing layout fails the step rather than skipping, since that is `package.bash`'s fault, not the box's.
	- Branch: round1027
	- Test case: defn2 (the step sends the layout as root and brings the `.pkg` into `dist/` and `checksums.txt`, a failed job and an empty `back/` fail, a down box skips without a second try, a missing layout fails) and defmv (the step's own check on the VM). defn2 failed against the old `cicd.bash`, and with the job sent as `bsdtest` or the checksum line left out.
	- Verified:
		- 20261026: On vmFreeBSD, 15.1-p4 with pkg 2.7.5, as root through the lock: `box-freebsd-pkg.bash` on the layout from `fFreeBSDPkgKit`, fed the beta.1 tarball. It made `zuid-1.0.0.b.1.pkg`, read back `FreeBSD:*:amd64` and the 5 files, installed it, and the installed command printed `zuid v1.0.0-beta.1 build dee5q`. An earlier try from a layout made by hand gave the same bytes from two `pkg create` runs.
		- 20261026: `pipeline-test.bash`, all passing, `test-ids.py check`, and shellcheck.
		- Not verified: `package.bash` writing the layout in a real run, and the step inside a full run.
	- Verified: 20261027, ddwmp, dee33 and defmv PASS in a plain `cicd/cicd.bash --backlog-run-full` on round1027 at 0ac99bd, with vmFreeBSD up.
	- Swept: `fPublish_Downloads` puts the `.pkg` in the FreeBSD x86_64 cell, after the tarball and the bare command. `install.bash` asks for the `.tgz` by exact name. The `.deb` and `.rpm` hold the same files. The other box steps don't change.

- An uninstall leaves the compiled module in the cache.
	- ID: 2026102407393000
	- Type: Enhancement
	- Status: Done
	- Priority [Feature|Enhancement] | Severity [Bug]: Low
	- Opened: 20261024-073930
	- Opened by: JC
	- Related IDs: 2026101410495989, 2026102618305629
	- Requirements  [Feature]:
		- `--uninstall` on a user install also removes the command's cache dir, such as `~/Library/Caches/zuid`, `~/.cache/zuid` or `%LOCALAPPDATA%\zuid`. It's about 8 MB.
		- A system install leaves other users' caches alone and says where they are.
	- Progress log:
		- 20261024: Seen on b26, after an install, one run and an uninstall.
		- 20261026: A user uninstall in both installers names the cache dir in its plan and removes it, from where `main.zig`'s `cacheRoot` puts it, `XDG_CACHE_HOME` included. A cache dir that's a link, or isn't a folder, is kept and said so.
		- 20261026: A system uninstall removes no cache, and its plan says where each user's is, with "safe to delete".
		- 20261026: `box-windows-test.ps1` runs the command once after each install, and checks the cache is there, then gone after a user uninstall and kept after a system one.
		- 20261026: `install.md` says what an uninstall does with the cache. Both installers are 1.4.0.
		- 20261026: `installer-test.bash` unsets `XDG_CACHE_HOME`. It was set on b23, so an uninstall case would have aimed at the real cache.
		- 20261026: Under bash 3.2 in the `bash:3.2` image, removing the cache fails on BusyBox `rm`, which has no `-x`. An install dir's `bin` goes through the same call, so it's filed as 2026102618305629.
	- Decisions:
		- 20261026: A system uninstall leaves the running user's cache too, not only other users'. The cache belongs to whoever ran the command, not to the install, and under sudo HOME may be someone else's. Leaving it costs 8 MB; removing it would only cost one compile.
		- 20261026: The whole `zuid` cache dir goes, not only `module-*.cwasm`, so a temp file from an interrupted save can't keep it. It goes through each installer's own guard.
		- 20261026: `install.bash` resolves the cache's parent physically, since `~/.cache` is often a link and FreeBSD's `/home` is one. `install.ps1` keeps a cache whose parent is a link, as its guard does.
		- 20261026: 1.4.0, not 1.3.3, since an uninstall now removes something it didn't before.
	- Branch: round1027
	- Test case: defpm and defpn (a user uninstall removes the cache), defpp and defpq (`XDG_CACHE_HOME` where the command reads it, and not on Darwin), defpr and defps (a cache dir that's a link stays), defpt and defpv (a system uninstall keeps them and says where), in `installer-test.bash` for each installer. All 8 failed against the old installers and pass after. deea5 has the Windows checks.
	- Verified: 20261026, `installer-test.bash` 107 of 107 with load about 22 on 32 CPUs, shellcheck, PSScriptAnalyzer on both changed `.ps1` files, and the quick gate.
	- Verified: 20261027, deea5 PASS on b29w and the macOS on GitHub step PASS, in a plain `cicd/cicd.bash --backlog-run-full` on round1027 at 0ac99bd. defpm to defpv PASS locally in the same run.
	- Swept: both installers' uninstall paths for user and system, `box-windows-test.ps1`'s uninstall, every uninstall call in `installer-test.bash` (GuardedRemoval, SystemTarget, ForeignLink, and Bash32 with its own HOME in the image), `install.md`'s cache paragraph, and the cache rules against `cacheRoot` in `main.zig`.

- The FreeBSD `.pkg`, built here when vmFreeBSD is taken or down.
	- ID: 2026102617244801
	- Type: Enhancement
	- Status: Done
	- Needs local test suite run?: none. Both halves of defp3 passed 20261027, as below.
	- Priority [Feature|Enhancement] | Severity [Bug]: Low
	- Opened: 20261026-172448
	- Opened by: JC
	- Parent ID: 2026101410500001
	- Prereq IDs: 2026102617244796
	- Target OS: FreeBSD
	- Requirements  [Feature]:
		- When the box step for 2026102617244796 skips, build FreeBSD's `pkg` from source in the container and run `pkg create` on the same layout.
		- Only if the result is sure to install on FreeBSD. For example, a test that builds one both ways and finds them the same, or installs the local one on the VM.
		- Once there's always a `.pkg`, add it to `default_releaseAssets`.
	- Progress log:
		- 20261026: Split. The `default_releaseAssets` part is 2026102618125670, which waits on a full run of this one.
		- 20261026: pkg 2.7.5 builds on the image's Debian, which upstream supports. Out of the box its `.pkg` differed from vmFreeBSD's in 3 ways, each fixed: no shared libraries listed, the builder's uid in each tar header, and no zstd frame checksum.
		- 20261026: The image builds pkg in a stage of its own, against libarchive 3.8.2, with the two `#ifdef __FreeBSD__` guards around the 65534 owner taken out. The FreeBSD box step then makes the `.pkg` here too, as defp3.
		- 20261026: Upstream patch and issue text drafted, in the private tree's `upstream/2026102617244801/`.
		- 20261027: The full run at 1a93c3a failed defp3: the box's `.pkg` wasn't the one made here. The only difference was each file's `mtime` in `+MANIFEST`, the time the files reached the box. `fBox_Pkg` copied the layout with `cp -R`, which drops the settled times, and pkg writes the real mtime there even with `SOURCE_DATE_EPOCH` set, which it uses only for the tar headers. The beta.1 compare sent the layout by tar, so it kept them. Now `cp -Rp`.
		- 20261027: Both sides also run `pkg create -t <stamp>` instead of setting `SOURCE_DATE_EPOCH`. pkg reads that only for the tar headers, while `-t` sets the manifest's times too, so the bytes no longer depend on the layout's mtimes. Same bytes as before for the same stamp.
		- 20261027: A second upstream draft, for `SOURCE_DATE_EPOCH` not reaching the manifest, is beside the first, as `manifest-mtime.*`.
		- 20261027: The full run at 0ac99bd passed dee33, defmv and defp3 with vmFreeBSD up. With the VM taken, the FreeBSD box step skips dee33 and defmv, and defp3 passes with the one made here in `dist/`.
		- 20261027: `ZUID_BOX_FREEBSD` set empty turns the whole step off, the same as `--no-remote`, so such a run has no `.pkg`. Only a box that is down or taken gets the one made here.
	- Decisions:
		- 20261026: "Sure to install" is the same bytes as `pkg create` on vmFreeBSD. A `.pkg` the box made is the check on the one made here on every run that has both, and a mismatch fails the step rather than picking one.
		- 20261026: Made here on every run with the image, not only when the box skips, so the check runs whenever it can. It costs about a second.
		- 20261026: pkg runs with `ABI=FreeBSD:14:<arch>` and `OSVERSION=1400000`. Those are pkg's own, so it reads the command's libraries as FreeBSD's. The package keeps the manifest's `FreeBSD:*:amd64`, and 14 or 15 there gave the same bytes.
		- 20261026: Without a box's to compare, the one made here has to be zstd. A pkg built without zstd falls back to xz quietly.
		- 20261026: libarchive 3.8.2 because it turned on the zstd frame checksum, which FreeBSD 15.1's has. A FreeBSD whose libarchive moves on can change the box's bytes, and defp3 would say so.
		- 20261026: The source patch is a `sed` in the Dockerfile, not a patch file, since the image tag is the Dockerfile's hash alone.
		- 20261026: The 2 upstream drafts aren't filed for now. The `sed` and `-t` cover both here, and nothing waits on pkg taking them.
		- 20261026: `--no-remote` turns the FreeBSD box step off, this check with it, so such a run has no `.pkg`.
		- 20261027: `-t` and `cp -Rp` both, not one. `-t` is pkg's own switch for a fixed time and covers any way the files travel; `-p` keeps the box's copy the same as the layout anyway.
	- Branch: round1027
	- Test case: defp4 (the box down puts the one made here in `dist/` and `checksums.txt`, an xz one or a failed `pkg create` fails, the box's bytes pass and others fail, no image skips) and defp3 (the step's own check). defp4 failed against the old `cicd.bash`, and with the zstd check taken out. defn2 also checks the layout reaches the box with its mtimes; it failed with `cp -R` and passes with `cp -Rp`.
		- 20261027: defp4's `pkg create` call changed from `SOURCE_DATE_EPOCH=1700000000 ... pkg create -M` to `pkg create -t 1700000000 -M`. It failed against 52dd605 and passes now.
	- Verified:
		- 20261026: From one layout of the beta.1 FreeBSD tarball, `pkg create` in the new image, as the caller's uid, and on vmFreeBSD (15.1-p4, pkg 2.7.5) gave the same sha256, `5917d77c...`.
		- 20261026: `pipeline-test.bash` 168 of 168, `test-ids.py check`, and shellcheck.
		- 20261027: The FreeBSD box step alone on vmFreeBSD, from the failed run's `dist/`: dee33, defmv and defp3 PASS, both `.pkg` files `405bf9c5...`. Again with `-t` and the layout's mtimes set to the time of the run: the same three PASS, and the same `405bf9c5...`. `pipeline-test.bash` 168 of 168, `test-ids.py check`, and shellcheck.
		- 20261027: Full run at 0ac99bd: defp3 PASS with vmFreeBSD up, its `.pkg` `2900e5d3...`.
		- 20261027: The FreeBSD box step alone at 89075c6, with that run's `dist/` less the box's `.pkg`, and vmFreeBSD taken: dee33 and defmv SKIP, defp3 PASS. The `.pkg` in `dist/` is zstd, 644, in `checksums.txt`, and `2900e5d3...`, the box's bytes. Again with every mtime in the layout set to the time of the run: the same.
		- Not verified: the box-left-out half inside a full run.
	- Swept: `fBox_PkgHome` now goes through the same `fPkg_IntoDist`, so both ways into `dist/` set 644 and add the checksum line. dee33, defn2 and dee9h still pass with the image left out, and a run with no FreeBSD tarball skips defp3 as dee33 does. design.md's FreeBSD `.pkg` line.
		- 20261027: Every `cp -R` in `cicd.bash` and the box scripts. Only `fBox_Pkg`'s feeds a package's bytes; the others copy headers, caches and debug files. `fBox_Release` sends the tarball whole, and `box-run.bash` sends and brings back by tar.
		- 20261027: Both `pkg create` calls, `fPkg_Here` and `box-freebsd-pkg.bash`, take `-t`. design.md's FreeBSD `.pkg` line still holds.
	- Acceptance signoff: Self-closed: defp3 passed with vmFreeBSD up in the full run at 0ac99bd, and with the VM taken, where the one made here matched the box's byte for byte.
	- Closed: 20261026-200836

- A Windows install leaves `PATH` to the user.

		- 20261014: Question: should `install.ps1` add the `bin` folder to the user or machine `PATH`, the way silkterm's does? Today it prints a hint. A machine `PATH` near 4 KB is a known trap on both test boxes.
		- 20261014: Answer: yes. A user install goes on the user `PATH`, a system one on the machine `PATH`, through the registry so `%VAR%` entries keep expanding. Skipped with a note past 2047 or 4095 characters. Uninstall takes it off. On b29w the user `PATH` came back byte for byte after an uninstall, once a trailing `;` was kept.
		- 20261025: On b29w as `collierjr`, an admin, `-Target system -Yes` put `C:\Program Files\zuid\bin` on the machine `PATH`, kept it `ExpandString`, and a second run changed nothing. `-Target system -Uninstall -Yes` gave the machine `PATH` back byte for byte, 4030 characters. The user install did the same for the user `PATH`, under both shells.
	- Branch: answers
	- Commit: 044ef7f
	- Test case: None in the harness, since it runs on Linux, where there is no registry. The b29w runs are the check.
	- Closed: 20261025-095700

	- ID: 2026101410495993
	- Type: Enhancement
	- Status: Done
	- Needs local test suite run?: Done. `cicd.bash --ci -q` passed, and so did `--container --only go --quick`.
	- Priority [Feature|Enhancement] | Severity [Bug]: Low
	- Opened: 20261014-105000

	- Verified: `go vet ./...`, `go test ./...`, and `go test -race` on TestConcurrentGenerate, TestErrorKinds and the new test. The new test fails with the cache keyed on one shared slot, both ways round: a raw-byte base passes after a good one, and a good one is refused after a raw-byte one.

- The macOS shared library exports all of Wasmtime.
	- ID: 2026093018112419
	- Type: Enhancement
	- Status: Done
	- Priority|Severity [Bug]: Low
	- Opened: 20260930-181124
	- Opened by: JC
	- Target OS: macOS
	- Requirements  [Feature]:
		- Only `zuid_*` exported, as on Linux.
	- Progress log:
		- 20261003: not tried. The Mac test host was unreachable.
		- 20261003: Done in `build.zig`, so the tests, `capi_smoke.c` and each slice in `package.bash` all get the same dylib. On a Mac it links the dylib again with Apple's linker, from `libzuid.a` plus Wasmtime, and installs it over Zig's copy. The symlinks stay as Zig made them.
		- 20261003: The export list is made from `lib/zuid.map` at build time, so there is no second copy to drift.
		- 20261003: Apple's linker signs the arm64 slice itself, so no `codesign` step. It defaults the compatibility version to 0.0.0, so the relink passes Zig's 1.0.0.
		- 20261003: With no Xcode command line tools, or when building for macOS off a Mac, the build warns and keeps Zig's dylib.
	- Decisions:
		- Zig's Mach-O linker ignores `lib/zuid.map` and has no exported symbols list. A relink with Apple's `ld -exported_symbols_list` would do it.
		- Low, because two-level namespace keeps the library's own calls bound to itself. `dcrex` passes on macOS.
	- Verified:
		- On b26, the full pipeline with `--package` passes. `dcrew` runs there now; the only skip left is the Linux-only glibc check.
		- The release dylib's two slices export 12 symbols each, all `zuid_*`. Each keeps `@rpath/libzuid.1.dylib` and macOS 13.0, and the arm64 slice is ad-hoc signed. `capi_smoke.c` runs against the release dylib.
		- The full Linux pipeline passes. The Linux build is unchanged.
	- Swept: every place the dylib is built on a Mac goes through `zig build`: the Zig stage, the C module stage and both slices in `package.bash`. Nothing else links it.
	- Branch: macexports
	- Commit: f3f5558, e011230, 9fd8271
	- Test case: `dcrew` runs on macOS now, and on both systems reads the allowed names from `lib/zuid.map` and checks every function `zuid.h` declares is exported. It failed on b26 with the old `build.zig` (828 stray exports) and passes with the fix. New `ddf3k` checks each slice of the release dylib under `--package`: exports, install name with its versions, macOS 13.0, and the arm64 signature. Its export check failed on a release built with the old `build.zig`. Its install name check rejects the first relink's dylib, which had compatibility version 0.0.0.
	- Acceptance signoff: Self-closed: the intent was clear, and the tests failed before the fix and pass after.
	- Closed: 20261003-143030
